# 02 - REPOSITORY AND FILE MAP

> **Evidence basis.** The PC worktree `D:/Projects/wt-sr-shadow-15sep` = commit **`e7bf477`** plus the uncommitted **`force_qty`** change set, which is byte-equivalent to the **TESTING VM** as delivered 17-Sep-2026 19:31 IST. Machine-side facts come from the preserved captures in `trading-system-sbx-snapshot/_machine/` (17-Sep 20:06 IST) and are marked RECORD-DERIVED where used. **PRODUCTION WAS NEVER READ** - nothing in this chapter describes production.
> **Evidence date:** 18-Sep-2026. **The system is retired**; this chapter describes the tree as it was. A defect noticed while mapping is recorded in chapter 22 and nowhere else.

## 2.1 What this chapter maps, and what it does not

The tree holds **1,465 files** (excluding `.git`, `.venv`, `__pycache__`, `.pytest_cache`, `node_modules`), of which **757 are Python modules** totalling **289,044 physical lines**.

**Mapped in full (SS2.5):** the **200 modules** reachable by static import from an entry point - `main` plus the **38** scripts named in `config/cron_registry.yaml` - **92,168 lines** in all. Every one has its own entry.

**Listed but NOT MAPPED (SS2.6):** the remaining **557** modules. `NOT MAPPED` is an honest state; a guessed one-line purpose is not.

**Method limit, stated once and applying to every entry below.** Reachability here is **static import reachability**, measured by an AST walk over every `import` and `from ... import` statement, with package `__init__` modules included. It is **not** observed runtime execution: a module can be imported and never called. Dynamic loading escapes the walk; the tree contains exactly three such sites - `core/config_auditor.py` (`importlib`, x2), `signals/signal_processor.py` (`__import__`, x2), `utils/startup_checks.py` (`importlib`, x1). `exec(` and `eval(` do not occur in any of the 344 non-test modules; controls for that zero: the same scan counts `import ` **3,008** times and an impossible token **0** times.

## 2.4 How to read an entry

Each entry carries **Role** (one label), **Lines** (physical), a **purpose** paragraph, **Imported by** and **Imports** (from the AST walk), **Key symbols** with their definition lines, **Inputs / Outputs / Side effects**, **Config keys read** and **DB tables** with the line of the access, **External calls**, **Paper vs live**, and **Unknowns**.

**Every line number below was verified against the file after it was written.** A claim whose cited line did not contain the cited symbol, key or table was **dropped, not corrected** - the drop counts are in SS2.7. The deterministic fields (line counts, importers, imports, log-call counts) come from the AST walk and a byte scan, not from prose.

## 2.5 The runtime modules, package by package

### `main` - 1 module, 4,329 lines

#### `main` - `main.py`

**Role:** entry-point / composition-root | **Lines:** 4,329 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

Single orchestration entry point: parses CLI args, refuses to start on a non-trading day or outside the 08:00-18:15 IST START window, loads config, opens the StateStore (the only sanctioned schema migrator, main.py:2241-2245), constructs and wires ~40 subsystems (broker adapter, fund manager, risk engine, screeners, signal processor, order placer/monitor/reconciler, EOD and MIS square-off, webhook server, health server), runs startup checks and a synchronous startup reconcile, then blocks on a shutdown event and tears everything down in reverse order. It is invoked as a service/cron process (main.py:4321-4329); the listed importers (scripts.sr_level_export and 9 test modules) import it as a module to call individual helpers rather than to boot the system.

- **Imported by (21):** `scripts.sr_level_export`, `tests.integration.test_q9_live_seed_mc1_wired`, `tests.unit.test_alert_delivery_phase1`, `tests.unit.test_c2_webhook_lockdown`, `tests.unit.test_delivery_config_independence`, `tests.unit.test_e4_event_loop_safety`, `tests.unit.test_eod_lifecycle_pipeline_aware`, `tests.unit.test_fix062_token_invalidation`, `tests.unit.test_fix130_eod_pre_alert`, `tests.unit.test_fix164_market_open_margin_sync` ... (+11)
- **Imports locally (83):** `alerts.critical`, `alerts.delivery`, `alerts.mis_squareoff_notifier`, `alerts.telegram_notifier`, `allocation`, `broker.clock_skew_probe`, `broker.cost_calculator`, `broker.order_monitor`, `broker.order_state_machine`, `broker.product_resolver`, `broker.rate_limiter`, `broker.slippage_engine` ... (+71)
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `concurrent`, `kiteconnect`, `requests`, `waitress`, `yaml`
- **Key symbols:** `main` (L2093) - Top-level boot: arg parse, --version, holiday guard, service-window guard, logging setup, instance lock, then delegates to _main_locked and always releases the lock. | `_main_locked` (L2201) - The whole boot body under the instance lock: config load, StateStore/KillSwitch, startup scenario, startup checks, subsystem construction, reconcile, thread starts, runtime wait, shutdown; returns the process exit code. | `_shutdown` (L1599) - Reverse-order teardown: drains a HARD_KILL flatten, emits the effect census, stops webhook/processor/daemons, cancels pending ENTRY orders, WAL-checkpoints and closes the DB. | `_parse_args` (L179) - Defines the CLI surface: --mode paper|live, --resume, --config, --status, --dry-run, --version, --interactive. | `required_startup_secrets` (L219) - Returns the env-var names that must be set in BOTH modes: ZERODHA_API_KEY_<acct>, ZERODHA_API_SECRET_<acct>, TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN, WEBHOOK_SECRET. | `_build_kite_client` (L381) - Live-only KiteConnect construction from os.environ ZERODHA_API_KEY / ZERODHA_ACCESS_TOKEN with a 1/50 HTTPAdapter pool. | `_make_paper_quote_provider` (L473) - Builds the paper-mode quote closure: reads the saved token file, applies config/symbol_aliases.yaml, paces calls at 0.35 s and caches Quotes for 3 s. | `compute_live_seed` (L2082) - Live startup capital = broker margins .net minus fund_manager.today_realized_pnl_carryover(day floor). | `_eod_self_exit_due` (L1230) - Pure-ish decision: returns (due, active) — due only past window_end, with no flatten in progress and zero positions that require this service; falls back to the product-blind count on any read error. | `_start_eod_self_exit_thread` (L1329) - Daemon thread that polls that decision after window_end, alerts on identity CONFLICT/UNRESOLVED/unprotected-delivery, and sets the shutdown event once flat. | `_resolve_position_pipeline` (L1121) - Resolves one position to INTRADAY/DELIVERY/CONFLICT/UNRESOLVED from strategy intent (primary) and ENTRY product via PRODUCT_TO_INTENT (second). | `_within_service_window` (L2012) - True iff now is in [08:00, SERVICE_START_CUTOFF) IST — the START guard only; the stop time is configured separately.
- **Inputs:** argv flags parsed at main.py:179-212 (--mode/--resume/--config/--status/--dry-run/--version/--interactive); config directory (default Path("config"), main.py:2107) loaded by load_all at main.py:2206; config/accounts.csv via AccountRegistry.load (main.py:2367, 2422); config/instruments.csv via InstrumentCache.load (main.py:2449); config/strategies/ via StrategyLoader.load_all_strategies (main.py:3169); config/symbol_aliases.yaml (main.py:510-515); data_store/session/zerodha_token.json (main.py:410-415, 2370, 2533-2562); data_store/trading_system.db opened with allow_migrate=True (main.py:2241-2245)
- **Outputs:** process exit code: 0 clean/holiday/out-of-window (2104, 2158, 2184, 4314), 1 lock or post-reconcile kill (2194, 3934), 3 startup-check/audit failures (2431, 2490, 2510, 2786, 2833, 2899, 3453, 3475), 4 HALT without --resume (2298), 5 config/arg failure (2100, 2209, 1906, 1909), 6 token missing (2378, 2382, 2560), 7/8 interactive cancel (1954, 1866), 9 unsupported broker (2580); session row INSERT OR REPLACE (main.py:625-633, called 2316 and 4209); system_events rows: CRASH_DETECTED (2285), CONFIG_DIFF (2665), STARTUP (4203), SHUTDOWN (1815); config snapshot row via snapshot_config (main.py:2611); candle rows via store.insert_candle on every candle close (main.py:3965-3976); Telegram messages and critical-email sentinels (273-282, 333-338, 346-355, 920, 1434, 1513, 2676, 4226); stdout: holiday banner (2136), out-of-window notice (2174), --status table (641-649), --dry-run report (2486), sizing mode (2883/2889), interactive banners (1853-1973, 4246-4250); HTTP servers: waitress webhook server (4023-4035) and healthcheck server on 8080 (4109)
- **Side effects:** Creates a module-level ThreadPoolExecutor at import time (main.py:865-867); Renames data_store/session/zerodha_token.json to .invalid on a broker auth failure (main.py:159); Mutates os.environ: ZERODHA_API_KEY (2369), ZERODHA_ACCESS_TOKEN (2371, 2584); Writes the holiday anti-flood sentinel logs/.holiday_notified_<date> (main.py:2141-2157); Acquires/releases a process-wide instance lock (main.py:2191, 2198); Installs SIGINT/SIGTERM handlers that set the global _shutdown_event (main.py:1570-1579); Mutates module globals _log (2094/2187), _broker_auth_failed (170-171), _shutdown_event (1573, 1630); Opens the SQLite DB with allow_migrate=True, i.e. may run schema migrations (main.py:2241-2245)
- **External calls:** KiteConnect(api_key=...) + set_access_token, live (main.py:386-387) and read-only paper market-data handle (414-415, 501-502); kite.quote(*instrument_keys) for paper quotes (main.py:546); kite.historical_data(...) through the rate-limited S&R fetch closure (main.py:432); kite.profile via TokenMonitor.profile_fn in live only (main.py:3399); HTTPS POST to api.telegram.org/bot<token>/sendMessage for the holiday notice (main.py:273-282); urllib GET with a TradingSystem/2.0 User-Agent for scanner/webhook startup probes (main.py:246-252); local HTTP GET of http://127.0.0.1:<port>/health after the webhook server starts (main.py:4039-4040); waitress.serve hosting webhook_receiver.app (main.py:4022-4035)
- **Config keys read:** `system.clock.warn_skew_sec` (L372), `broker_limits.timeouts.read_sec` (L385), `system.special_sessions` (L456), `nse_holidays.holidays` (L443), `system.trading_hours.service_window_end` (L2219), `system.trading_hours.market_open` (L2238), `system.kill_switch.api_failure_threshold` (L2255), `system.kill_switch.enable_auto_trip` (L2256), `system.capital.emergency_exit_buffer_pct` (L2258), `system.trading_hours.entry_start` (L2329), `system.product_map` (L2357), `broker_costs` (L2358), `scan_webhook_map` (L2466), `chartink_scanners` (L2467)
- **DB tables:** `session` (L625), `trades` (L905), `webhook_audit` (L1671)
- **Log calls by level:** debug 1 | info 45 | warning 18 | error 58 | critical 38
- **Paper vs live:** Divergent throughout: kite_client is None in paper and built from env in live (2360-2383); the adapter gets paper_mode plus a token-file quote_provider only in paper (2398-2402); SlippageEngine is wired paper-only (2520-2524); paper_capital is late-bound from accounts.csv (2599) and re-synced after rehydrate (2839-2845) while live seeds capital from compute_live_seed (2766); TokenMonitor.profile_fn is a lambda: None in paper (3399); BrokerClockSkewProbe is constructed live-only (3941-3953); stale paper orders are cancelled only in paper (3983-3986); TelegramNotifier gets paper_mode=(args.mode == "paper") (2642); effect_telemetry.set_mode(is_paper) drives the composition assertion's consequence (2593).

#### `core.account_registry` - `core/account_registry.py`

**Role:** account-config-registry | **Lines:** 360 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Loads and validates config/accounts.csv into frozen AccountRow records (one primary Zerodha account in v2), and separately provides never-raising helpers that read just the is_primary row for alert subject tags and for the NAME of the env var holding that account's API key/secret. Imported by alerts.cron_alerts, core.evidence_contract, main, ops.control_tower.reporter and five scripts.

- **Imported by (32):** `alerts.cron_alerts`, `core.evidence_contract`, `main`, `ops.control_tower.reporter`, `scripts.alert_watcher`, `scripts.auto_refresh_token`, `scripts.check_cron_drift`, `scripts.cron_officer`, `scripts.cron_report_render`, `scripts.cron_watchdog` ... (+22)
- **Imports locally (1):** `core.exceptions`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `AccountRow` (L48) - frozen dataclass of the ten CSV columns; the *_env fields hold env var NAMES, not credentials | `AccountRegistry` (L75) - in-memory list + account_id index of the parsed rows | `AccountRegistry.load` (L91) - classmethod parser: missing file -> ConfigMissingError, missing column / bad row / no-or-multiple primary / enabled-with-paper_capital<=0 -> ConfigSchemaError | `AccountRegistry.primary` (L215) - returns the is_primary row, raising ConfigSchemaError if none | `AccountRegistry.get` (L224) - account_id lookup, KeyError on miss | `AccountRegistry.all_accounts` (L236) - copy of rows in load order | `AccountRegistry.get_enabled_accounts` (L240) - rows with enabled=True in load order | `AccountRegistry.count` (L244) - number of rows loaded | `primary_account_tag` (L277) - cached primary account_id for alert subjects; returns 'UNKNOWN' on any failure | `_read_primary_row` (L302) - minimal CSV read of the is_primary row as a dict, {} on any exception | `reset_primary_account_tag_cache` (L321) - clears the module-level tag cache (tests only) | `primary_api_key_env` (L347) - env var NAME for key (347), NAME for secret (352), and the key VALUE read from os.environ (357-360)
- **Inputs:** path to accounts.csv (load, line 91; the helpers default to <repo>/config/accounts.csv at 308-310); the ten required CSV columns (110-114); os.environ, read by name at line 360
- **Outputs:** AccountRegistry / list[AccountRow] / AccountRow; primary account_id string or 'UNKNOWN' (296); env var name strings or '' (349, 354), API key value or '' (360)
- **Side effects:** reads the accounts.csv file (118, 312); mutates the module-global _primary_tag_cache (298, 324); one WARNING log when more than one account is enabled (205-209); reads process environment (360)
- **External calls:** none - no broker or HTTP call; only local CSV reads and an os.environ lookup (360)
- **Config keys read:** `accounts.csv` (L309), `accounts.csv` (L309), `accounts.csv` (L309), `accounts.csv` (L309), `accounts.csv` (L309), `accounts.csv` (L309), `accounts.csv` (L309), `accounts.csv` (L309)
- **Log calls by level:** warning 1
- **Paper vs live:** The CSV carries a paper_capital column (line 66) and load() requires it to be > 0 for any enabled account (163-169), but this module never consumes it - no behaviour here branches on paper vs live.
- **Unknowns:** The real contents of config/accounts.csv on either machine (which account is primary, how many rows are enabled).; Whether ConfigMissingError/ConfigSchemaError accept the arbitrary kwargs passed here (core.exceptions is not in this batch).; Whether any caller actually uses totp_secret_env from this module (the comment at 61-65 points at scripts/auto_refresh_token.py).

#### `core.candle_math` - `core/candle_math.py`

**Role:** pure-indicator-math | **Lines:** 456 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Pure candle math over the shared sr_detector.models.Candle type: true range series and ATR (Wilder or SMA), session VWAP, EMA series/latest, Wilder ADX, Wilder RSI, and a timeframe resample that buckets base bars onto a grid anchored to caller-supplied session bounds. No I/O, no clock, no config. Consumed by core.daily_stats, regime.engine, sr_detector.zone_builder, the v3_chain modules and two tests.

- **Imported by (8):** `core.daily_stats`, `regime.engine`, `sr_detector.zone_builder`, `tests.unit.test_candle_math`, `tests.unit.test_v3_chain`, `v3_chain.pb01_entry`, `v3_chain.pb01_runner`, `v3_chain.runner`
- **Imports locally (1):** `sr_detector.models`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `true_range_series` (L54) - TR per candle in input order; TR_0 degenerates to high-low | `atr` (L83) - ATR over `period`; needs period+1 candles, drops the degenerate first TR, raises ValueError on bad period/method | `session_vwap` (L136) - volume-weighted typical price; skips non-positive volume bars, returns None when total volume is 0 | `ema_series` (L165) - SMA-seeded EMA series, [] when fewer than `period` values | `ema` (L184) - latest EMA or None; thin wrapper over ema_series | `adx` (L191) - Wilder ADX, None when fewer than 2*period+1 candles; reuses the TR formula inline (213-217) | `rsi` (L250) - Wilder RSI over closes; 100.0 when no losses, 50.0 when perfectly flat (287-288) | `resample` (L335) - buckets base candles into minute or daily bars, flagging partial and empty grid slots | `ResampleResult` (L310) - frozen result: bars, partial_bucket_starts, empty_bucket_starts, plus the partial_last property (327) | `SessionBounds` (L303) - either a fixed (open, close) time tuple or a callable date -> (open, close) | `ATR_WILDER` (L50) - method and timeframe string constants (50-51, 298)
- **Inputs:** Sequence[Candle] reading .high/.low/.close/.volume/.open/.ts; integer periods (atr 85, ema_series 165, adx 191 default 14, rsi 250 default 14); target_tf as positive int minutes or 'day'/'daily' (337, 378); session_bounds tuple or callable (338, 386)
- **Outputs:** list[float] (true_range_series), Optional[float] (atr, session_vwap, ema, adx, rsi), list[float] (ema_series); ResampleResult with the new Candle list and the two flag tuples (452-456)
- **Side effects:** none - no I/O, no globals, no clock; the only runtime project import is the function-local `from sr_detector.models import Candle` at line 376
- **Paper vs live:** The header states the module is 'deterministic and identical in paper and live by construction' (line 13), and no branch in the file reads a mode.
- **Unknowns:** The Candle dataclass's real field set and tz convention (sr_detector.models is not in this batch), so the duck-typed reads cannot be confirmed against it.; Whether callers pass session bounds derived from core.market_windows as the docstring instructs.; Whether any caller relies on session_vwap's skip-zero-volume behaviour (151-152).

#### `core.closure_source` - `core/closure_source.py`

**Role:** vocabulary-constants | **Lines:** 113 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The single vocabulary module for position-closure attribution: it declares the five closure_source values (who closed a position), the five exit_mechanism values (how the order reached the broker), the frozensets grouping them, and the four-rung evidence precedence ladder. Everything else in the file is contract prose - the first principle (suppress a CRITICAL only on positive evidence), the rung-4 expiry rule and the contradiction rule - held as comments, not code. #### `core.config_auditor` - `core/config_auditor.py`

**Role:** config-validation | **Lines:** 858 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

One rule engine, seven check groups (A-G), that audits a loaded SystemConfig for contradictions, resurrected deleted YAML keys, insane capital-relative percentages, active slippage overrides, launch-phase params, stale component constructor defaults and cross-field timing/leverage/tick errors. Each rule yields an AuditFinding at PASS/INFO/WARN/BLOCK; the only hard action is ConfigAuditReport.raise_if_blocked(). Called from the startup path (core.config_loader via audit_system_config, groups 'ACG') and the 08:30 pre-flight (scripts.preflight.checks.config_sanity via audit_app_config, all groups), plus main and three tests.

- **Imported by (7):** `core.config_loader`, `main`, `ops.control_tower.aggregator`, `scripts.preflight.checks.config_sanity`, `tests.unit.test_config_auditor`, `tests.unit.test_ni11_c2_has_a_delivery_arm`, `tests.unit.test_phase4_trade_type_gate`
- **Imports locally (2):** `orders.order_placer`, `strategies.control`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `yaml`
- **Key symbols:** `Severity` (L45) - PASS / INFO / WARN / BLOCK enum, ranked by _SEV_RANK (53) | `ConfigContradictionError` (L68) - ValueError subclass raised by raise_if_blocked so a Pydantic validator wraps it as a ValidationError | `AuditFinding` (L76) - frozen finding: group, stable code, severity, message, metrics | `ConfigAuditReport` (L89) - finding collection with by_severity/blocks/warns/infos, worst_in_group (111), verdict (118), one_line (131) and raise_if_blocked (142) | `_group_a_contradictions` (L187) - A1 force_intraday_only+DELIVERY, A2 trade_type domain, A4 structure-exit vs trailing SL, A5 delivery-without-conditional-allocation (all BLOCK), A3 zero-strategies-trade (WARN) | `_group_b_single_source` (L326) - raw-YAML regression guards B1-B4 (WARN) plus B5 scoring threshold ordering (BLOCK) | `_max_effective_multiplier` (L401) - largest tier x perf multiplier, clamped by the sizer's hard 2x ceiling constant (398) | `_group_c_capital_relative` (L416) - C1 daily-loss band, C2/C2d the position-cap-vs-effective-concentration ladder, C3 risk band, C4 risk vs concentration, C5 cumulative risk | `_group_d_active_overrides` (L533) - lists non-empty slippage override maps (INFO) and re-uses orders.order_placer.validate_slippage_overrides for typo/extreme WARNs | `_group_e_launch_phase` (L576) - emits one INFO per LAUNCH_PHASE_PARAMS row (registry at 162) | `_group_f_stale_default` (L593) - introspects constructor defaults in _STALE_DEFAULT_GUARDS (175) against config and reports compared/required/unresolved outcomes | `_group_g_cross_field` (L672) - G1 entry_end vs square-off gap, G2 leverage, G3 min_tick_size, G5 per-strategy window overlap

#### `core.config_loader` - `core/config_loader.py`

**Role:** config-schema-and-loader | **Lines:** 2,611 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Defines the strict Pydantic v2 schema for the eight non-strategy YAML config files and the single loader that reads, hashes and validates them (load_all, line 2552), returning one AppConfig (line 2515) that main.py and the subsystems listed in the manifest's imported_by are handed by dependency injection. It is the boot-time fail-closed gate: every bound, ordering rule and "no silent default" contract for trading hours, capital/leverage, sizing, risk caps, alerts, reconciler and the shadow modules is expressed here as a field validator, and a failure raises before any StateStore or broker handle exists. It also computes a SHA-256 per file into AppConfig.file_hashes (line 2586) for restart-time change detection.

- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `pydantic`, `yaml`

#### `core.config_snapshotter` - `core/config_snapshotter.py`

**Role:** persistence / config-snapshot writer | **Lines:** 217 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Serializes the already-resolved runtime config to a canonical (sorted-key, compact) JSON string, sha256-hashes it, and writes one `config_snapshots` row per (snapshot_date, config_hash) so a later report can read "what config was live on date X" from the DB instead of from YAML (lines 4-12). Idempotent: an identical (date, hash) row makes it skip; a different hash on the same day inserts a second row (19-22). Its only importer on the live path is main (manifest hint).

#### `core.config_validator` - `core/config_validator.py`

**Role:** startup config-usage gate | **Lines:** 274 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

A module-level singleton that records every config value a module actually reads (`get`), registers every leaf of five AppConfig sub-trees as 'expected' (`register_all_from_app_config`), and at the end of boot asserts each registered key was read and that no read value drifted from the loaded one (`validate_all`). Imported by main and tests.core.test_config_validator.

- **Imported by (2):** `main`, `tests.core.test_config_validator`
- **Imports locally (1):** `core.exceptions`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `ConfigUsage` (L58) - dataclass holding key, expected_value, actual_value, accessed, access_count, accessor_modules | `ConfigValidator` (L68) - the tracker class; one module-level instance is the API | `reset` (L81) - clears _tracked and _validated (test hook) | `disable` (L86) - sets _enabled False, turning every method into a no-op/pass-through | `enable` (L90) - re-enables tracking | `register_expected` (L94) - records a key + its loaded value as expected, unaccessed | `get` (L104) - pass-through tracker: marks the key accessed, bumps the count, records the module, WARNs 'CONFIG_DRIFT' when expected != value (128), and creates a new tracked entry for an unregistered key (133) | `register_all_from_app_config` (L144) - recursive pydantic walk registering every leaf under a dotted path | `get_usage_report` (L185) - returns accessed/unaccessed/drift lists plus four counts | `validate_all` (L221) - the gate: unaccessed keys WARN (255) or raise in strict mode (250); any drift ALWAYS raises ConfigValidationError (263) | `config_validator` (L274) - the process-wide singleton instance every module imports
- **Inputs:** get(key: dotted str, value, module: str) (line 104); register_expected(key, value) (line 94); register_all_from_app_config(app_config) -- reads .system/.broker_costs/.broker_limits/.slippage/.scoring (lines 179-183); validate_all(strict: bool = False) (line 221)
- **Outputs:** get returns the same value it was handed (line 142); get_usage_report returns a dict with accessed/unaccessed/drift entries and total/accessed/unaccessed/drift counts (lines 211-219); raises ConfigValidationError with unaccessed_keys (line 250) or drift_keys (line 263); log lines: CONFIG_DRIFT warning (128), CONFIG_UNACCESSED warning (255), CONFIG_DRIFT_DETECTED error (259), summary info (268)
- **Side effects:** mutates the module-level singleton's _tracked dict (lines 77, 102, 133) -- global process state; logging at debug/info/warning/error; no DB, file or network access
- **Config keys read:** `system` (L179), `broker_costs` (L180), `broker_limits` (L181), `slippage` (L182), `scoring` (L183)
- **Log calls by level:** debug 1 | info 1 | warning 2 | error 2

#### `core.daily_stats` - `core/daily_stats.py`

**Role:** indicator computation (pure) | **Lines:** 74 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

One pure function (M-S4) that computes four scorer inputs -- prev_close, avg_volume_20d, atr14, rsi14 -- from DAILY candles, using only candles dated strictly before the target trading_date (the no-lookahead guarantee). It composes the existing core.candle_math.atr/rsi rather than adding parallel indicators, and returns None for any stat the history cannot support. Consumed by scripts.forward_shadow_record and sr_shadow.pipeline (manifest hint).

- **Imported by (3):** `scripts.forward_shadow_record`, `sr_shadow.pipeline`, `tests.unit.test_daily_stats`

- **Inputs:** candles: Sequence[Candle] assumed to be DAILY and in chronological order (lines 35, 56-57); trading_date: str used as a string comparison boundary (line 36, 58); atr_period=14, rsi_period=14, vol_window=20 keyword-only (lines 38-40)
- **Outputs:** a dict with exactly {'prev_close','avg_volume_20d','atr14','rsi14'} (lines 60, 69-74); all four None when no prior candle exists (60); each stat independently None when history is insufficient (65, and per candle_math's own None contract)

Substrate only' (lines 14-15) is still true, given the manifest lists sr_shadow.pipeline and scripts.forward_shadow_record as importers; Whether atr/rsi tolerate a candle with a missing ts (they receive the same unfiltered `prior` list)

The forward evidence contract (Batch 1, 10-Sep-2026): a fixed-shape JSONL record for every signal decision at three capture points (P1_ACCEPT / P2_REJECT / P3_SCREEN), a per-capture-point (and per-P3-verdict-shape) table deciding which context is REQUIRED, OPTIONAL or N/A, a sha256 fingerprint over the DEPLOYED .py bytes instead of a git ref, and an EvidenceRecorder that appends one line per decision and is wrapped so it can never raise into the trading pipeline. Imported by main and tests.unit.test_evidence_contract.

#### `core.logger` - `core/logger.py`

**Role:** logging infrastructure | **Lines:** 472 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The single logging facade: every module calls get_logger(__name__), and main.py calls setup_logging() once to attach four daily file sinks (system/trades/reconciler as JSON lines, debug as plain text) plus a WARNING+ stdout sink. File writes go through a QueueHandler drained by a background QueueListener thread so a slow or full disk cannot freeze a trading thread (431-445). It also caps third-party HTTP loggers at INFO because urllib3's DEBUG request line contains the Telegram bot token in the URL path (82-104).

- **Imported by (60):** `alerts.cron_alerts`, `alerts.telegram_notifier`, `broker.clock_skew_probe`, `broker.order_monitor`, `broker.rate_limiter`, `broker.token_monitor`, `broker.zerodha_adapter`, `capital.drift_handler`, `capital.fund_manager`, `data.live_feed` ... (+50)
- **Imports locally (2):** `core.exceptions`, `core.time_authority`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `SafeJSONEncoder` (L120) - JSON encoder that never raises: scrubs NaN/Inf to None and stringifies datetime/Decimal/Exception/numpy (135-172) | `_JsonFormatter` (L179) - emits one compact JSON object per record in the fixed L9 field order (189-219) | `_PlainFormatter` (L222) - human-readable format with IST timestamps for the debug sink (231-233) | `_SystemFilter` (L240) - passes INFO+ to the system sink | `_TradesFilter` (L246) - passes only records carrying signal_id / trade_id / order_id | `_ReconcilerFilter` (L256) - passes only loggers named order_reconciler* / order_monitor* (259) | `get_logger` (L266) - returns (and memoises in _loggers) a stdlib Logger; attaches no handlers | `TradeContext` (L277) - LoggerAdapter that merges bound IDs into every call's extra (283-287) | `bind_trade` (L290) - wraps a logger with non-None signal_id/trade_id/order_id so records route to the trades sink | `log_exception` (L316) - logs at the level derived from TradingSystemError.SEVERITY, else ERROR, always with exc_info and exc.context (326-338) | `cap_third_party_http_loggers` (L341) - sets urllib3/requests/httpx/httpcore to INFO so credential-bearing DEBUG URLs never reach the debug file | `setup_logging` (L354) - removes prior handlers, mkdirs the log dir, opens the four dated files, starts the QueueListener and sets root to DEBUG
- **Inputs:** get_logger(name) (266); bind_trade(log, signal_id=, trade_id=, order_id=) (290); log_exception(log, exc) (316); setup_logging(log_dir: Path = Path("logs")) (354)
- **Outputs:** logs/system_<YYYY-MM-DD>.log, logs/trades_<date>.log, logs/reconciler_<date>.log, logs/debug_<date>.log (filename built at line 394); WARNING+ lines on stdout (419-421); Logger / TradeContext objects
- **Side effects:** creates the log directory (385) and opens four append-mode files (395); starts a background QueueListener thread (445) and stops it (371, 471); mutates global logging state: root level DEBUG (450), root handlers (452), third-party logger levels (351); module-level globals _loggers / _active_handlers / _queue_listener (111-113)
- **Log calls by level:** info 1
- **Unknowns:** whether any entry point calls cap_third_party_http_loggers() without setup_logging(), as the docstring at 347 anticipates; the actual log directory used in production (caller-supplied default Path("logs") relative to cwd, line 366); whether the 10000-slot queue (435) ever saturates in production — this file has no drop counter

#### `core.market_windows` - `core/market_windows.py`

**Role:** time-window authority | **Lines:** 333 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Stateless NSE clock arithmetic: given a caller-supplied `now`, it answers whether the market is open, whether entries are allowed (globally and per strategy), whether the EOD square-off or the entry cutoff has passed, and what the next trading day is. It holds no clock and does no I/O — every time value is injected at construction (50-71) — and is consumed by main, orders.eod_squareoff, orders.order_reconciler, orders.tgt_retry_manager, broker.token_monitor and several cron scripts.

- **Inputs:** constructor times entry_start/entry_end/market_open/market_close/eod_squareoff/eod_entry_cutoff, a holidays set and a special_sessions dict (50-59); a timezone-aware `now: datetime` on every method; a StrategyConfig-like object with entry_start_time / entry_end_time (169-170); is_market_day / is_broker_api_available take an optional dt and an optional holidays set (275, 288)

#### `core.migrations` - `core/migrations.py`

**Role:** schema migration engine | **Lines:** 444 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Idempotent SQLite schema migrations for the state store, imported only by core.state_store. Because CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS cannot add a CHECK, a FOREIGN KEY or a STORED generated column to an existing table, this module performs the documented 12-step rebuild by extracting the table's CURRENT definition straight out of schema.sql text, copying the intersecting columns into a temp table and swapping (254-331). It also performs the one-off relocation of the analytics tables from trading_system.db into the attached analytics database (338-393).

- **Imported by (3):** `core.state_store`, `tests.unit.test_migrations`, `tests.unit.test_p0_live_day1_fixes`
- **Imports locally (0):** none
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `MIGRATION_TABLES` (L115) - version -> list of tables to rebuild at that step (v25 through v45) | `MigrationError` (L136) - raised on any migration failure; the DB is left rolled back | `_extract_block` (L144) - paren-balanced scan that pulls whole top-level statements out of the schema text | `extract_create_table` (L170) - returns the single CREATE TABLE block for a table, raising if absent or duplicated (177-179) | `extract_indexes_for` (L183) - returns every CREATE [UNIQUE] INDEX ... ON <table> block | `_normalize_ddl` (L194) - strips comments/IF NOT EXISTS/quotes and collapses whitespace so live and target DDL compare equal | `_live_table_sql` (L210) - reads the stored DDL for a table out of sqlite_master | `_table_columns` (L217) - non-generated column names via PRAGMA table_xinfo, excluding hidden 2/3 | `_nongen_columns_in` (L230) - same, qualified by schema name ('main' or 'analytics') | `_table_in_schema` (L241) - True if schema.sql still defines the table (relocated tables do not) | `_rebuild_table_from_schema` (L254) - the 12-step rebuild: FK pragma off, BEGIN IMMEDIATE, build tmp, copy intersecting columns, DROP, RENAME, recreate indexes, foreign_key_check, COMMIT (295-328) | `relocate_analytics_tables` (L338) - per-table transaction that clears analytics.<t>, copies main.<t> into it and drops it from main (377-384)
- **Inputs:** run_migrations(conn, schema_text, from_version, target_version, log) (400-406); relocate_analytics_tables(conn, tables, log) (338-342); the text of core/schema.sql as a string (parsed, never read from disk by this module)
- **Outputs:** int count of tables rebuilt (444) / relocated (393); raises MigrationError (177, 179, 285, 316, 371, 436); INFO log lines at 330, 392, 440 and a DEBUG line at 430
- **Side effects:** DDL and DML against the live SQLite connection: DROP TABLE (309, 383), ALTER TABLE RENAME (310), INSERT ... SELECT (306-308, 379-382), DELETE FROM analytics.<t> (378), CREATE INDEX (312); toggles PRAGMA foreign_keys off and back on around the rebuild (297, 328); explicit BEGIN IMMEDIATE / COMMIT / ROLLBACK transactions (299, 319, 322, 377, 384, 387); logging only; no network, no files
- **External calls:** sqlite3 connection execute of DDL/DML only
- **DB tables:** `signals` (L116), `trades` (L116), `orders` (L116), `kill_switch_state` (L116), `screener_results` (L117), `smart_tgt_state` (L117), `shadow_trades` (L117), `reconciliation_log` (L118), `innings` (L118), `fm_ledger` (L123), `webhook_audit` (L123), `order_execution_log` (L126)
- **Log calls by level:** debug 1 | info 3
- **Unknowns:** the actual live schema_version of any deployed database (this file only maps versions to tables); whether the caller re-applies schema.sql after run_migrations (that ordering lives in core/state_store.py, not here); whether the `analytics` database is in fact ATTACHed when relocate_analytics_tables runs — stated as a precondition at 350-352 but never checked

#### `core.mis_blocklist` - `core/mis_blocklist.py`

**Role:** learned-rejection store | **Lines:** 160 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Learns which symbols the broker itself refuses MIS (intraday) orders for, by matching Zerodha's literal HTTP-400 phrase 'mis orders are currently blocked' (35, 55), and persists {SYMBOL: date} to a JSON file with a re-test TTL (default 5 days). orders.order_placer records a block on that rejection and main wires the store; the screener side asks is_blocked() before letting a future MIS-intent signal through. Deliberately no DB schema — the store is one JSON file (11).

- **Imported by (3):** `main`, `orders.order_placer`, `tests.unit.test_mis_blocklist`
- **Imports locally (2):** `core.effect_telemetry`, `core.time_authority`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `_MIS_BLOCK_PHRASE` (L35) - the broker's literal phrase, matched case-insensitively as a substring | `is_mis_block_rejection` (L38) - True if str(exc) or exc.context's rejection_reason/reason/message contains the phrase (45-55) | `MisLearnedBlocklist` (L58) - thread-safe, restart-safe {symbol: last_blocked_date_iso} map with a TTL | `MisLearnedBlocklist.__init__` (L65) - takes path, ttl_days, logger and an injectable today_fn; loads the JSON at construction (79-82) | `MisLearnedBlocklist.record_block` (L85) - upper-cases the symbol, stamps today's date, persists under the lock, then bumps effect telemetry and logs INFO (89-100) | `MisLearnedBlocklist.is_blocked` (L102) - True only if the symbol is present AND days_since < ttl_days; fail-open on a corrupt date (109-114) | `MisLearnedBlocklist.info` (L116) - (last_blocked_date_iso, days_since) or (None, None) for logging | `MisLearnedBlocklist._days_since` (L126) - calendar-day delta, None when the stored string is not an ISO date | `MisLearnedBlocklist._load` (L133) - reads the JSON at construction; any failure starts empty and logs a WARNING (139-145) | `MisLearnedBlocklist._persist_locked` (L147) - atomic write: mkdir parents, write <path>.tmp, os.replace (150-155)
- **Inputs:** constructor: path, ttl_days=5, logger, today_fn (65-71); record_block(symbol) (85), is_blocked(symbol) (102), info(symbol) (116); is_mis_block_rejection(exc) — any exception object (38)
- **Outputs:** the JSON blocklist file, keys sorted, indent 2 (153); bool / tuple return values; INFO on a recorded block (97), WARNING on a load failure (142), ERROR on a persist failure (158); one effect-telemetry counter increment per recorded block (95)
- **Side effects:** creates the parent directory and writes the JSON file plus a .tmp sibling, then os.replace (150-155); reads the JSON file at construction (136); increments the 'mis_blocklist' effect-telemetry handle (77, 95); holds a threading.Lock around the in-memory dict (80)
- **Config keys read:** `ttl_days` (L68)
- **Log calls by level:** info 1 | warning 1 | error 1
- **Unknowns:** the actual store path and ttl_days used in production (both are caller-supplied); whether any symbol has ever been recorded (the file contents are not in this module); whether the screener-side lookup described at 12-13 is actually wired (only main and orders.order_placer import this module per the manifest)

#### `core.state_store` - `core/state_store.py`

**Role:** persistence | **Lines:** 3,355 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The single SQLite gateway for this system's persistent runtime state: it owns connection setup (WAL, synchronous FULL, foreign_keys ON, 30s busy_timeout — lines 107-113), the schema-version gate and migration permission gate on open (lines 398, 420-427), an explicit BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction context manager (line 531), and roughly 100 hand-written query/write helpers used by capital.fund_manager, capital.kill_switch, capital.risk_engine, main, orders.eod_squareoff, orders.order_manager, orders.order_reconciler, reports.daily_report, reports.daily_trade_review and scripts.backfill_closure_source_w8 (manifest importers). Its helpers encode the system's operational definitions rather than plumbing only — e.g. which trade statuses count as 'executed' for the daily cap (_EXECUTED_TRADE_STATUSES, line 734), how a DELIVERY trade is identified (ENTRY-leg orders.product = 'CNC', line 874), and the atomic once-only status flips that decide whether capital is released twice (lines 1794, 2423). The module deliberately contains no business math, no alerting and no broker access; the only non-SQLite act is a best-effort CRITICAL sentinel file written when a migration is refused (lines 344-352).

- **Imported by (169):** `capital.fund_manager`, `capital.kill_switch`, `capital.risk_engine`, `main`, `orders.eod_squareoff`, `orders.order_manager`, `orders.order_reconciler`, `reports.daily_report`, `reports.daily_trade_review`, `scripts.backfill_closure_source_w8` ... (+159)
- **Imports locally (6):** `alerts.critical`, `core`, `core.db_connect`, `core.exceptions`, `core.migrations`, `core.time_authority`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `EXPECTED_SCHEMA_VERSION` (L102) - Module constant = 45; the version the code demands, compared against schema_meta on every open. | `StateStore` (L150) - The whole public surface; shareable across threads because each thread gets its own sqlite3.Connection via threading.local (line 192). | `StateStore.__init__` (L172) - Takes db_path/schema_path plus keyword-only allow_migrate and market_open, creates the parent directory (195) and runs _initialize_schema (198). | `StateStore._refuse_migration` (L326) - Logs CRITICAL MIGRATION_REFUSED (339), writes a critical sentinel file into the DB's parent directory (344-352) and raises MigrationNotPermitted (355). | `StateStore._initialize_schema` (L357) - Reads schema.sql, inits+attaches analytics.db, refuses a NEWER DB (398), gates then runs migrations (426-440), then executescript(schema_sql) (446) and re-verifies the version (450). | `StateStore.transaction` (L506) - @contextmanager yielding a cursor inside BEGIN IMMEDIATE (531) / COMMIT (533), with ROLLBACK on any exception (536) and the original exception re-raised (541). | `StateStore.fetch_all` (L576) - Generic SELECT helper returning List[sqlite3.Row]; with fetch_one (564) and execute (549) it is the only raw-SQL door callers get. | `StateStore.count_active_positions` (L639) - One atomic COUNT over trades in OPEN/PARTIAL/PENDING_FILL (652-655), replacing two separate counts that could miss a trade mid-transition. | `StateStore.get_all_open_trades` (L1432) - OPEN/PARTIAL trades LEFT JOINed to their ENTRY order for product and broker order id (1443-1465); returns False on any exception and never raises.

**Role:** strategy metadata accessor + registry I/O | **Lines:** 142 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Single canonical accessor for a strategy's LONG/SHORT, read from the declared StrategyConfig.direction field in config/strategies/*.yaml rather than from a `_long`/`_short` name suffix (5-7, 69-70). #### `core.time_authority` - `core/time_authority.py`

**Role:** clock + skew authority | **Lines:** 395 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The single source of 'now' for the whole system (every module calls now_ist() instead of datetime.now(), 94-99) and the accumulator for broker clock skew. Layer 3 hands it broker response timestamps; it keeps a rolling window (default 10 samples) and fires injected WARN/ALERT/HALT callbacks by tier without ever importing the kill switch (7, 21-22). assert_clock_at_startup() is the one-shot boot gate that raises ClockSkewTooLarge above 30 s.

- **Imported by (177):** `alerts.critical`, `alerts.telegram_notifier`, `broker.order_monitor`, `broker.token_monitor`, `broker.zerodha_adapter`, `capital.fund_manager`, `capital.kill_switch`, `capital.risk_engine`, `capital.shadow_engine`, `capital.strategy_governor` ... (+167)
- **Imports locally (1):** `core.exceptions`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `now_ist` (L94) - current time in the fixed UTC+05:30 IST zone built at line 59 | `ist_timezone` (L102) - returns the IST tzinfo object for callers that format timestamps | `today_ist` (L107) - today's date as a YYYY-MM-DD string (used for log filenames) | `now_ist_iso` (L112) - current IST time as an ISO-8601 string | `configure` (L125) - updates thresholds and, only for explicitly-passed arguments, the three callbacks; the _UNSET sentinel (122) stops a thresholds-only call from wiping the critical brake (159-164) | `record_broker_skew` (L171) - appends one skew reading, computes the mean of absolute values, picks a tier and fires the matching callback outside the lock (213-248) | `assert_clock_at_startup` (L263) - single-reading boot gate: raises ClockSkewTooLarge past startup_max_sec, else seeds the window (285-301) | `get_skew_status` (L308) - snapshot dict of last/avg skew, sample count, tier and a copy of the thresholds | `reset` (L349) - clears the window and all three callbacks and restores default thresholds (tests / cold start) | `SkewResult` (L368) - __slots__ value object returned by record_broker_skew (371-387) | `_DEFAULT_THRESHOLDS` (L66) - warn 2 s, alert 5 s, halt 30 s, startup_max 30 s, min_samples_for_halt 3, window 10
- **Inputs:** configure(thresholds, on_critical_skew, on_alert_skew, on_warn_skew) (125-130); record_broker_skew(broker_timestamp, local_ref_ts=None) (171-174); assert_clock_at_startup(broker_timestamp) (263)
- **Outputs:** IST datetime / date-string / ISO-string (94, 107, 112); SkewResult (250-256); skew seconds as float from the startup check (301); the status dict (340-346); raises ClockSkewTooLarge (286); fires the injected callbacks with (avg, reason) (243-248)
- **Side effects:** mutates module globals under a lock: _skew_window, _thresholds, the three callbacks (80-87, 151-164, 213, 298, 356-361); invokes caller-supplied callbacks, which elsewhere trigger a soft-kill (243-248); no logging, no DB, no files, no network
- **Config keys read:** `warn_sec` (L225), `alert_sec` (L224), `halt_sec` (L223), `min_samples_for_halt` (L222), `startup_max_sec` (L283), `window_size` (L155)
- **Unknowns:** what thresholds main.py actually configures and which callbacks it wires; whether the HALT tier has ever fired in production (no counters or logs here); whether broker timestamps arrive timezone-aware — the module assumes IST for naive input (195, 277)

#### `broker.auth_recovery` - `broker/auth_recovery.py`

**Role:** error-classifier / alert-text builder | **Lines:** 98 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Stdlib-only helpers that turn a broker auth failure into an operator-actionable classification and alert text. `classify_broker_auth_error` decides IP-allowlist vs token-expiry vs unknown; `get_public_ip` fetches the VM's current public IP; `build_ip403_alert_body` composes the CRITICAL alert body naming the exact IP to allowlist. Imported by broker.token_monitor, capital.kill_switch and two preflight checks.

- **Imported by (7):** `broker.token_monitor`, `capital.kill_switch`, `scripts.preflight.checks.broker`, `scripts.preflight.checks.security`, `tests.unit.test_auth_recovery`, `tests.unit.test_h13_token_monitor_relatch`, `tests.unit.test_kill_switch`
- **Imports locally (0):** none
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `_IP_MARKERS` (L28) - Tuple of 8 lowercase substrings that mark an IP-allowlist/permission failure. | `_TOKEN_MARKERS` (L40) - Tuple of 6 lowercase substrings that mark a token/session-expiry failure. | `classify_broker_auth_error` (L50) - Lowercases str(error) and returns 'IP_NOT_ALLOWLISTED', 'TOKEN_EXPIRED' or 'AUTH_UNKNOWN'; IP markers are tested first (line 60). | `get_public_ip` (L67) - Tries api.ipify.org then checkip.amazonaws.com over urllib with a timeout, returning the stripped body or a literal 'unknown — check the VM's public IP manually' (line 79); never raises. | `build_ip403_alert_body` (L82) - Builds a multi-line alert body embedding public_ip twice plus a 160-char truncation of raw_error (line 96).
- **Inputs:** classify_broker_auth_error(error: object) — any object whose str() carries the broker message (line 50); get_public_ip(timeout: float = 5.0) (line 67); build_ip403_alert_body(public_ip: str, raw_error: str = '') (line 82)
- **Outputs:** One of three classification strings (lines 61, 63, 64); A public-IP string or the 'unknown …' sentence (lines 78, 79); A newline-joined alert body string (line 98)
- **Side effects:** Outbound HTTPS GET to api.ipify.org / checkip.amazonaws.com inside get_public_ip (lines 71-73). No DB writes, no file writes, no logging, no threads.
- **External calls:** urllib.request.urlopen against https://api.ipify.org and https://checkip.amazonaws.com (lines 71-73)

#### `broker.clock_skew_probe` - `broker/clock_skew_probe.py`

**Role:** background-probe / thin driver | **Lines:** 141 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

A daemon-thread driver that every probe_interval_sec calls adapter.get_server_time(), brackets it with two time_authority.now_ist() reads, and hands the mid-point plus the broker timestamp to time_authority.record_broker_skew(). It deliberately owns no tier logic, no thresholds and no callbacks — the module docstring (lines 34-39) states time_authority does all classification and the HALT→soft_kill dispatch. Imported by main and its unit test.

- **Imported by (2):** `main`, `tests.unit.test_clock_skew_probe`
- **Imports locally (3):** `core.config_loader`, `core.exceptions`, `core.logger`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `BrokerClockSkewProbe` (L53) - The whole module: constructor takes adapter, time_authority, ClockSkewProbeConfig and an optional logger (lines 56-68). | `BrokerClockSkewProbe.start` (L70) - Returns early when config.enabled is false (line 71) or the thread is alive (line 74); otherwise starts a daemon thread named 'broker_clock_skew_probe' (lines 78-83). | `BrokerClockSkewProbe.stop` (L89) - Sets the stop event and joins the thread with a 2.0 s timeout (lines 90-92). | `BrokerClockSkewProbe._run_loop` (L94) - Probe-then-wait loop: calls _probe_once, catches every exception and keeps looping, then waits probe_interval_sec on the stop event (lines 97-107). | `BrokerClockSkewProbe._probe_once` (L109) - t_before → adapter.get_server_time() → t_after, computes mid_local (line 133) and calls record_broker_skew (lines 138-141); returns without recording on auth/429/timeout/unknown broker errors (lines 113-131).
- **Inputs:** Constructor: adapter, time_authority, config: ClockSkewProbeConfig, logger (lines 56-62); Per cycle: adapter.get_server_time() (line 112) and two time_authority.now_ist() reads (lines 110, 132)
- **Outputs:** No return values. The only product is the record_broker_skew(broker_timestamp=…, local_ref_ts=…) call on lines 138-141.; Log lines: 'clock_skew_probe disabled by config' (72), 'already running' (75), 'started, interval=%ds' (84-87), and four skip/error lines (102, 114, 120, 123, 127)
- **Side effects:** Starts a daemon thread (lines 78-83); Mutates time_authority's rolling skew state indirectly via record_broker_skew (line 138); Network call through adapter.get_server_time() (line 112)
- **External calls:** adapter.get_server_time() — a broker round trip (line 112)
- **Config keys read:** `enabled` (L71), `probe_interval_sec` (L86), `probe_interval_sec` (L107)

#### `broker.cost_calculator` - `broker/cost_calculator.py`

**Role:** cost-model / pure calculator | **Lines:** 353 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Computes itemized Zerodha transaction cost for a single order leg (brokerage, STT, exchange txn, GST, SEBI, stamp duty) from an injected BrokerCostsConfig, with every component quantized to 2 dp ROUND_HALF_UP, plus round-trip convenience wrappers. Consumed by zerodha_adapter, order_placer, order_reconciler, cnc_gtt_monitor and main. `round_trip_costs_or_zero` is the fail-open wrapper the backstop close paths use so a cost failure never blocks a capital release.

#### `broker.order_monitor` - `broker/order_monitor.py`

**Role:** fill-poller / order-lifecycle driver | **Lines:** 1,351 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The live fill-polling daemon: every poll_interval_sec it snapshots its watch list, calls adapter.get_order_history() per order, maps the Zerodha status onto the OrderStateMachine, and publishes OrderFilled / OrderPartiallyTerminated / OrderStatusChanged on the event bus. It also owns the cancel-side policies: fill timeout, immediate cancel on first ENTRY partial, the pending-R:R cancel, the force-close-time sweep, orphan detection after 3 empty histories, and the auth/API circuit breakers that stop the thread. Imported by main, order_placer and eod_squareoff.

#### `broker.order_state_machine` - `broker/order_state_machine.py`

**Role:** state-machine | **Lines:** 231 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The in-memory, thread-safe authority for order state. It owns a 9-state set, a one-way transition table in which the four terminal states have no exits, and refuses every illegal move with InvalidTransitionError. Callers pass only the target state — the machine reads from_state itself. It persists nothing and logs nothing; order_monitor, zerodha_adapter, eod_squareoff and main all drive it.

Two shared safety helpers for the flatten paths, written for FIX-190 Bug A so a second flatten of an already-closed position cannot fire another same-side order and open a naked opposite position. `determine_close_direction` derives the close side from the ACTUAL signed broker quantity (and returns (None, 0) only when the broker confirms flat), and `cancel_co_bracket` is the single broker gesture that closes a CO position. #### `broker.rate_limiter` - `broker/rate_limiter.py`

**Role:** throttle / token bucket | **Lines:** 272 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Client-side token-bucket throttle in front of every Zerodha call, with four independent buckets (order, quote, historical, margins), each with its own lock and its own capacity/refill rate from BrokerLimitsConfig. acquire() blocks up to max_wait_sec then raises; try_acquire() is non-blocking; penalize() freezes a bucket for the adapter's 429 backoff. All refill arithmetic uses time.monotonic() so it is NTP-immune (RL8).

(+1)

- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`

#### `broker.slippage_engine` - `broker/slippage_engine.py`

**Role:** paper-fill slippage model | **Lines:** 186 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Applies adverse per-liquidity-tier slippage to paper-mode synthesised fills so paper P&L does not systematically beat live (audit CFG-6: slippage_model.yaml was loaded but never consumed). apply() resolves a tier from InstrumentCache, moves the price against the trader by tiers[tier].slippage_bps, and tick-rounds away from the price — unless the slip is smaller than half a tick, in which case rounding is skipped. Also hosts the three tick-rounding helpers that zerodha_adapter and smart_tgt_manager import.

#### `broker.token_monitor` - `broker/token_monitor.py`

**Role:** background watchdog | **Lines:** 235 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Background watchdog that polls the broker's profile endpoint every check_interval_sec during market hours to confirm the Zerodha access token is still valid. On a genuine token-invalid signal it logs CRITICAL, sends a CRITICAL Telegram notification and fires the injected on_expiry callback (main wires it to soft_kill) — once per episode, re-armed by the next valid check. It deliberately never re-logins, never closes positions and never hard-kills. Imported by main and two unit tests.

- **Imported by (3):** `main`, `tests.unit.test_fix128_token_monitor`, `tests.unit.test_h13_token_monitor_relatch`
- **Imports locally (4):** `broker.auth_recovery`, `core.logger`, `core.market_windows`, `core.time_authority`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `TokenMonitor` (L39) - Constructor takes profile_fn, on_expiry, logger, check_interval_sec=1800, paper_mode, market_open/close 'HH:MM' strings, notifier and mode (lines 56-67). | `TokenMonitor.start` (L95) - No-op in paper mode (lines 97-99); otherwise starts the daemon thread named 'token-monitor' (lines 103-108). | `TokenMonitor.stop` (L114) - Sets the stop event, joins with a 5 s timeout, logs token_monitor.stop (lines 116-119). | `TokenMonitor.check_now` (L121) - The testable core: True in paper mode; calls profile_fn and on success resets the expiry latch (lines 128-145); on exception classifies and either fires expiry or logs a transient warning (lines 146-165). | `TokenMonitor._is_market_hours` (L167) - Delegates to is_within_market_hours(now_ist().time(), open, close); returns True unconditionally when either bound is None (lines 170-172). | `TokenMonitor._is_token_expiry` (L174) - True when the exception type is named 'TokenException' or classify_broker_auth_error returns 'TOKEN_EXPIRED'; a classifier failure returns False so it cannot manufacture an expiry (lines 187-193). | `TokenMonitor._handle_expiry` (L195) - Latches _expiry_fired, sends the CRITICAL '[mode] TOKEN EXPIRED' notification, then invokes on_expiry, logging CRITICAL if the callback raises (lines 197-224). | `TokenMonitor._check_loop` (L226) - Wait-then-check loop: waits check_interval_sec, skips outside market hours, then calls check_now (lines 228-235).
- **Inputs:** Constructor args on lines 57-66, notably profile_fn (the broker profile call), on_expiry (the soft-kill callback), notifier and the 'HH:MM' market window strings; Per cycle: the result or exception of profile_fn() (line 132); now_ist() for the market-hours test (line 172)
- **Outputs:** check_now returns True when valid, False on any exception (lines 145, 165); A CRITICAL notification with severity/title/body/source_module='token_monitor' (lines 203-213); The on_expiry() callback invocation (line 218); Log lines: start (109), stop (119), check_ok DEBUG (133), token_recovered INFO (140), token_expired_or_invalid CRITICAL (154), transient_check_error WARNING (160), on_expiry_callback_failed CRITICAL (221), outside_market_hours DEBUG (233)

#### `broker.zerodha_adapter` - `broker/zerodha_adapter.py`

**Role:** broker-adapter | **Lines:** 2,520 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The single wrapper around the kiteconnect SDK: every broker call in the system goes through this class, and the module docstring states no other module imports kiteconnect (lines 5-7, ZA1 at 10-11). It validates order arguments, takes a rate-limiter token per category, resolves a semantic intent to a broker product code, registers/transitions the order state machine, calls kite, and translates kiteconnect exceptions into the system's BrokerError family; it also carries a full in-memory paper simulator (orders, fills, positions, GTTs, holdings, capital) so the same public surface serves paper mode. Its importers are main, broker.order_monitor, orders.eod_squareoff, orders.order_protocol_co, orders.order_protocol_limit and scripts/tests (manifest hint).

The adapter is still the only kiteconnect user **inside the service's trade path**; the claim fails for the scripts and for `main.py`'s own session bootstrap. Registered in chapter 22.

#### `data.candle_store` - `data/candle_store.py`

**Role:** candle builder | **Lines:** 368 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Builds minute OHLC candles from LTP ticks and is the single source of intraday candle data for main.py's consumers. on_tick() accumulates per instrument_token; a daemon timer thread fires on wall-clock interval boundaries and closes every accumulator, emitting immutable CandleData to registered callbacks (66-73). Audit 3.4 constraints are structural here: OHLC comes only from LTP, never from the tick's exchange `ohlc` field (173), and closing is clock-driven, not tick-driven.

- **Imported by (8):** `main`, `tests.unit.test_candle_store`, `tests.unit.test_fix135_tick_volume`, `tests.unit.test_fix142_final_cleanup`, `tests.unit.test_h9_candle_offbyone_landmine`, `tests.unit.test_phase17_batch3`, `tests.unit.test_phase19_batch3`, `tests.unit.test_smart_tgt_manager`
- **Imports locally (1):** `core.time_authority`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `CandleData` (L25) - frozen candle record: token, symbol, OHLC, volume, close ts, interval, is_synthetic (27-36) | `_Accumulator` (L40) - mutable per-symbol window state; update() folds one tick's LTP and volume in (50-63) | `CandleStore` (L66) - the store; MAX_HISTORY 500 candles per token (77) under one lock (88) | `CandleStore.start` (L110) - starts the daemon 'candle-store-timer' thread | `CandleStore.stop` (L120) - sets the stop event (does not join) | `CandleStore.set_token_map` (L128) - injects the instrument_token -> symbol map (from InstrumentCache.token_map()) | `CandleStore.gc_sweep` (L133) - drops accumulators and history for tokens no longer in the instrument cache, returning the count removed (146-168) | `CandleStore.on_tick` (L170) - accumulates LTP+volume into the current clock-derived window; creates the accumulator on first tick (187-195) | `CandleStore.register_on_candle_close` (L197) - idempotent callback registration | `CandleStore.get_candles` (L211) - last N closed candles for a token, oldest-first | `CandleStore.mark_reconnect` (L219) - discards all partial accumulators after a feed gap, keeping closed history (221-227) | `CandleStore._close_candles` (L253) - snapshots and clears accumulators, emits synthetic flat candles for silent mapped tokens (280-287), appends to history and fires callbacks outside the lock (354-368)
- **Inputs:** constructor: logger, candle_interval_sec=60 (79-83); on_tick(instrument_token, ltp, ts, volume=0) (170); set_token_map(token_map) (128); gc_sweep(valid_tokens) (133); register_on_candle_close(fn) / unregister_on_candle_close(fn) (197, 203); mark_reconnect(ts) (219); get_candles(token, n) (211)
- **Outputs:** CandleData objects delivered to every registered callback (363-366); in-memory per-token history deque capped at 500 (358); int removed count from gc_sweep (168); INFO on gc_sweep (163), WARNING on mark_reconnect (224) and on a synthetic-only streak (300), ERROR when a callback raises (368)
- **Side effects:** starts a daemon thread named 'candle-store-timer' (113-118); mutates internal state under a lock: _accum, _history, _token_map, _on_close_cbs (89-92); invokes caller callbacks synchronously on the timer thread (366); no DB writes, no files, no network in this module

#### `data.live_feed` - `data/live_feed.py`

**Role:** broker market-data adapter | **Lines:** 790 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Manages the Zerodha KiteTicker WebSocket for main.py: connects, tracks subscriptions for auto-resubscribe, and distributes normalized LTP ticks through one bounded queue drained by a single immortal consumer thread (audit 3.3: no thread-per-callback). It also runs a tick-age watchdog thread that detects a half-open socket and a dead consumer thread, escalating through the injected kill switch and Telegram notifier (102-131, 698-790).

- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `kiteconnect`, `weakref`

#### `screening.entry_gate` - `screening/entry_gate.py`

**Role:** pullback-gate worker | **Lines:** 537 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

A price-pullback holding pen: WatchEntry snapshots sit in an in-memory watchlist while a daemon thread polls LTP every poll_interval_sec and releases an entry on PRICE_HIT (ltp inside entry_price +/- tolerance_pct), TIMEOUT (elapsed >= timeout_sec) or QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE (3 consecutive quote failures), invoking the injected on_release callback. It persists to and rehydrates from `gate_state` so a mid-session restart resumes the same watches. main.py constructs it (main.py:3874) and starts it (main.py:4010); the telemetry comment at lines 122-123 records that nothing has ever fed it.

- **Imported by (5):** `main`, `tests.integration.test_hardening_scenarios`, `tests.unit.test_entry_gate`, `tests.unit.test_evidence_contract`, `tests.unit.test_signal_processor`
- **Imports locally (2):** `core.effect_telemetry`, `core.time_authority`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `concurrent`
- **Key symbols:** `WatchEntry` (L54) - frozen dataclass holding the full signal context (ids, prices, tolerance, timeout, strategy, tier, scanner, intent, added_at naive IST, extras dict) | `EntryGate` (L83) - the gate: watchlist plus poll thread plus worker pool | `EntryGate.__init__` (L108) - takes quote_fn, logger, state_store, on_release, poll_interval_sec=5.0, worker_count=2; raises ValueError when poll_interval_sec <= 0 (117) | `start` (L144) - rehydrates from gate_state, then launches the 'eg-worker' pool and the 'eg-poll' daemon thread | `rehydrate_from_gate_state` (L179) - rebuilds WatchEntry objects from state_store.get_all_gate_state(); skips duplicates and malformed rows; returns the count | `stop` (L241) - sets the stop event, joins the poll thread (5s), shuts the pool down without waiting, then sleeps 3s (254-255) | `clear_all` (L260) - FIX-046 EOD hook: clears the in-memory watchlist and calls state_store.clear_all_gate_state() | `add` (L288) - inserts a WatchEntry (ValueError on duplicate id), persists gate_state and sets signals.status = GATE_WAITING | `remove` (L350) - idempotent removal by signal_id; returns True/False | `size` (L368) - current watchlist size under the lock; watchlist() at 363 returns a snapshot list | `_check_one` (L411) - per-entry worker body: timeout check, quote fetch with consecutive-failure counting, symmetric tolerance-band test | `_release` (L474) - atomic remove -> release_gate_state(signal_id, GATE_RELEASED_*) -> log -> on_release(entry, reason)

#### `screening.hard_gate` - `screening/hard_gate.py`

**Role:** gate-rules (pure) + rescale helpers | **Lines:** 466 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Two things in one file: (a) `HardGate`, the pre-scoring binary battery used by the V3 paths in secondary_screener (at-circuit, circuit-proximity, freshness; liquidity is an explicit no-op at line 170), and (b) the pure re-scale helpers plus the V3 shadow/playbook gate functions (RR, HTF, EXTREME, CONFIRM, PULLBACK) shared with v3_chain.* and the offline parity recompute script so there is one implementation. `circuit_proximity_reason` is the relocated body of secondary_screener's former private rule (header comment lines 14-16).

#### `screening.quality_scorer` - `screening/quality_scorer.py`

**Role:** scoring | **Lines:** 150 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Turns step_executor's per-step raw scores (0.0-1.0) into a composite 0-100 score, a HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW tier and a pass/fail flag, using weights and thresholds from ScoringConfig. Consumed by secondary_screener.screen() and by main.py. Scoring is proportional (FIX-042): a missing step is excluded from the denominator rather than scored 0, so infrastructure jitter does not penalise a signal.

#### `screening.retest_monitor` - `screening/retest_monitor.py`

**Role:** parked-candidate monitor + divert decision | **Lines:** 441 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The WAIT_FOR_RETEST holding pen for the S&R V2 path. RetestDiverter.maybe_divert (called from signals/signal_processor.py:1082) parks a candidate whose entry sits inside a HIGH-confidence contra zone BEFORE any sizing or reservation, so no capital is held; RetestMonitor then polls fresh 1m candles per parked symbol on a daemon thread and advances the pure sr_detector.retest_confirm state machine, handing a CONFIRMED candidate to signal_processor.continue_from_retest and dropping a REJECTED one. State is persisted to `retest_state` and rehydrated at boot (main.py:3819-3832).

#### `screening.step_executor` - `screening/step_executor.py`

**Role:** screening-step runner | **Lines:** 423 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Runs the ten screening steps for one signal, each a method returning a raw 0.0-1.0 score, submitting each step to a shared single-worker thread pool with a per-step timeout and capturing exceptions per step so run_all() never raises. Consumed by secondary_screener (both the legacy 10-step path and the V3 8-step path via exclude_steps) and by main.py.

- **Imported by (10):** `main`, `screening`, `screening.secondary_screener`, `tests.crash_test.ct_day2_isolated`, `tests.integration.conftest`, `tests.unit.test_hard_gate`, `tests.unit.test_ms3_step_timeout_bounds_caller`, `tests.unit.test_phase19_batch3`, `tests.unit.test_secondary_screener`, `tests.unit.test_step_executor`
- **Imports locally (1):** `core.time_authority`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `concurrent`

#### `signals.entry_throttle` - `signals/entry_throttle.py`

**Role:** rate-limiter | **Lines:** 125 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

An in-memory, thread-safe entry-rate limiter used by signals/signal_processor.py (constructed at signal_processor.py:265, called at signal_processor.py:1463, :2278 and :2583) to stop bursts of entry placements. Three gates are evaluated global-first inside one lock: a minimum seconds gap between any two placed entries (:78), a rolling-window burst cap (:86), and a per-symbol cooldown (:98). admit() is deliberately a single atomic check-AND-record call (:65, record at :110-113) rather than a split check/record pair, so concurrent worker threads cannot all pass a check before any of them records.

- **Imported by (2):** `signals.signal_processor`, `tests.unit.test_entry_throttle`
- **Imports locally (0):** none
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `ThrottleResult` (L31) - Frozen result record: allowed, reason text, and category (""|min_gap|burst|per_symbol). | `EntryThrottle` (L37) - The limiter itself; holds a lock, last-entry timestamp, a deque of recent placements and a per-symbol map. | `EntryThrottle.__init__` (L40) - Keyword-only construction of the three gate settings plus an injectable clock (default time.monotonic). | `EntryThrottle.enabled` (L62) - True when at least one of min_gap, burst_max or per_symbol cooldown is positive. | `EntryThrottle.admit` (L65) - Atomic check-and-record; returns allowed=True and records the placement, or allowed=False with the blocking gate's category and increments that gate's counter. | `EntryThrottle.metrics` (L117) - Returns the four counters (entries_throttled_min_gap/burst/per_symbol, entries_admitted) for /metrics.
- **Inputs:** Constructor keywords min_gap_sec, burst_window_sec, burst_max, per_symbol_cooldown_sec, clock (:40-48); admit(symbol) — an optional symbol string (:65)
- **Outputs:** ThrottleResult(allowed, reason, category) from admit() (:72, :83, :92, :104, :115); A dict of four counters from metrics() (:120-125)
- **Side effects:** Mutates only its own in-memory state under self._lock: _last_entry, _recent deque, _per_symbol_last map and _counts (:110-114); No DB, no file, no network, no logging

#### `signals.signal_processor` - `signals/signal_processor.py`

**Role:** signal-admission-orchestrator | **Lines:** 2,756 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Worker pool that drains the receiver's signal_queue and runs each queued signal through the admission pipeline: PROCESSING status write, kill-switch / entry-window / 60s-age / shadow-inning pre-flight, scanner->strategy lookup via scan_webhook_map, strategy-control verdict, secondary screener, entry+SL derivation, optional momentum fresh-LTP re-anchor, position sizing, then (inside fund_manager.portfolio_lock) per-strategy cap + one-trade-per-symbol-direction + risk approve + capital reserve, then entry throttle and order_placer.place(). It owns orchestration and status bookkeeping only; every decision is delegated to injected objects (screener, sizer, risk_engine, fund_manager, kill_switch, order_placer). Its importers are main (the live wiring) and ten unit/integration test modules; it also exposes three alternative entry points used by other components: continue_from_gate (EntryGate PRICE_HIT, line 2063), continue_from_retest (RetestMonitor CONFIRMED, line 2428) and admit_prepared/reject_prepared (the PortfolioAllocator enforce worker, lines 1699 and 1759).

#### `signals.webhook_receiver` - `signals/webhook_receiver.py`

**Role:** entry-point | **Lines:** 1,207 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The Flask HTTP ingress for Chartink scanner webhooks, used by main.py. It authenticates (HMAC header or ?token=), rate-limits per source IP, validates the JSON payload, resolves symbol aliases, de-duplicates, enforces queue backpressure and per-signal expiry, inserts a row into `signals`, and pushes a (signal_id, scanner_name, symbol, price, triggered_at) tuple onto the shared signal_queue for signal_processor (:1047-1049). It also exposes an authenticated GET /health (:364) and a separate EOD route (:751) that hands DAILY/EOD alerts to a watchlist capture worker instead of the intraday queue, and writes one `webhook_audit` row per POST regardless of outcome (:531, :1126).

- **Imported by (11):** `main`, `tests.integration.conftest`, `tests.integration.test_hardening_scenarios`, `tests.unit.test_c2_webhook_lockdown`, `tests.unit.test_c6_auth_collapse`, `tests.unit.test_e4_event_loop_safety`, `tests.unit.test_fix074_numeric_cast`, `tests.unit.test_fix134_backpressure`, `tests.unit.test_phase17_batch2`, `tests.unit.test_v3_eod_route` ... (+1)
- **Imports locally (4):** `core.account_registry`, `core.ids`, `core.time_authority`, `utils.cron_heartbeat`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `cachetools`, `flask`, `hmac`, `yaml`
- **Key symbols:** `_PerIpRateLimiter` (L69) - Thread-safe per-source-IP token bucket with lazy eviction of buckets idle >60 s (:102). | `_strip_account_prefix` (L111) - Strips a leading `<primary account tag><-|_>` from an already-normalised scan_name so per-account Chartink alert names still match the URL path segment. | `WebhookReceiver` (L141) - The gateway; its __init__ refuses to construct when require_hmac=True without a secret (:177-182) and starts the in-flight sweeper thread (:220). | `WebhookReceiver._authenticate` (L298) - The single auth decision for both routes: sha256= HMAC over the given payload, else reject if require_hmac, else the ?token= bearer. | `WebhookReceiver._load_symbol_aliases` (L258) - Loads config/symbol_aliases.yaml once at startup into an upper-cased alias map; missing/broken file yields {} (passthrough). | `WebhookReceiver._handle_webhook` (L477) - Outer POST wrapper: shutdown 503, per-IP 429, calls _process_request, converts any exception to a 500, and always writes the webhook_audit row in `finally`. | `WebhookReceiver._process_request` (L536) - The gate order and parse: auth → unknown-scanner 404 → EOD route → kill-switch 403 → backpressure 503 → entry-window 403 → JSON/field/scan_name/triggered_at parsing → per-symbol loop. | `WebhookReceiver._handle_eod` (L751) - Self-contained EOD path: parses the payload and calls eod_capture.submit() per symbol, never touching the intraday queue; records a cron heartbeat. | `WebhookReceiver._sanitize_payload_for_storage` (L857) - Redacts the literal secret, any token=<value>, and the webhook_url field from the copy persisted to signals.webhook_payload. | `WebhookReceiver._process_signal` (L892) - Per-symbol pipeline: price validation, expiry, atomic in-flight claim, TTL dedup, fingerprint, INSERT into signals, queue push, and the QUEUE_FULL / STORE_ERROR / re-queue recovery paths. | `WebhookReceiver.release_in_flight` (L1102) - Public hook signal_processor calls when it finishes a symbol; update_heartbeat (:1110) is the liveness hook. | `WebhookReceiver._run_sweeper` (L1182) - Daemon loop that every 60 s evicts in-flight symbols with no heartbeat for 60 s and logs CRITICAL per eviction.
- **Inputs:** Constructor: signal_queue, state_store, config, market_windows, kill_switch, logger, secret_token, eod_capture (:152-162); eod_capture can be late-bound via set_eod_capture (:1156); POST /webhook/<scanner_name> (:406) — JSON body with required stocks, trigger_prices, triggered_at (:609) and optional scan_name (:620); comma-separated symbol and price strings (:670-671); Headers/query: X-Webhook-Signature (:333), ?token= (:334); request.remote_addr (:479); GET /health (:364); config/symbol_aliases.yaml (:265)

#### `strategies.control` - `strategies/control.py`

**Role:** gating resolver (pure) | **Lines:** 117 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

One pure function, strategy_will_trade (67), is the sole authority for 'will this strategy trade today?', imported by both the entry gate (signals/signal_processor) and the status/pre-flight surfaces so the gate and the displayed table cannot disagree. It applies four layers in order: the per-strategy enabled switch (81), the force_intraday_only breaker against a DELIVERY intent (90), and the master trade_type against the strategy intent (100, 107).

- **Imported by (8):** `core.config_auditor`, `main`, `scripts.strategy_status`, `signals.signal_processor`, `tests.unit.test_p0_live_day1_fixes`, `tests.unit.test_pb01_registration`, `tests.unit.test_phase4_trade_type_gate`, `tests.unit.test_slice2_strategy_control`
- **Imports locally (0):** none
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `VALID_TRADE_TYPES` (L33) - the set {INTRADAY, DELIVERY, BOTH}; defined here and referenced nowhere in the tree | `CAUSE_OK` (L41) - machine-readable cause codes (41-44) so the gate can label a trade_type mismatch without string-matching the reason | `Verdict` (L48) - frozen dataclass will_trade / reason / product / cause, cause defaulting to CAUSE_OK (64) | `strategy_will_trade` (L67) - keyword-only trade_type and force_intraday_only; returns a Verdict; disabled first (81), then the breaker (90), then trade_type vs intent (100, 107), else WILL TRADE (116)
- **Inputs:** strategy: anything with .intent (76) and .enabled (77); trade_type: str — INTRADAY | DELIVERY | BOTH (70); force_intraday_only: bool (71)
- **Outputs:** a Verdict with will_trade, a human-readable reason string, the effective product, and a cause code (82, 91, 101, 107, 116)
- **Side effects:** none — no I/O, no logging, no mutation (declared at 26)
- **Config keys read:** `intent` (L76), `enabled` (L77), `trade_type` (L70), `force_intraday_only` (L71)
- **Paper vs live:** none visible — the docstring states paper and live evaluate identically (line 26)
- **Unknowns:** how each caller maps a cause to a reject label (the mapping lives in signals/signal_processor); where trade_type and force_intraday_only are read from config; whether a strategy object lacking .intent can reach here (getattr defaults to None at 76)

#### `strategies.loader` - `strategies/loader.py`
