#### `alerts.telegram_notifier` - `alerts/telegram_notifier.py`

**Role:** alert-transport | **Lines:** 914 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The tier-aware alert transport: formats an alert as HTML and POSTs it to the Telegram Bot API, with routing by severity — INFO/WARN drop on failure, ERROR appends to logs/failed_alerts.log, CRITICAL writes a sentinel via alerts.critical FIRST and then sends, with a direct SMTP email fallback if Telegram failed for every channel (docstring lines 4-27; code lines 386-393, 493-523). Almost every alerting caller in the system routes through it (main, order_reconciler, reports.daily_report, ops.control_tower, and six scripts).

- **Imported by (42):** `alerts.cron_alerts`, `main`, `ops.control_tower.aggregator`, `ops.control_tower.runner`, `orders.order_reconciler`, `reports.daily_report`, `scripts.alert_watcher`, `scripts.approve_ssh_keys`, `scripts.auto_refresh_token`, `scripts.backup_retention` ... (+32)
- **Imports locally (3):** `alerts.critical`, `core.logger`, `core.time_authority`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `email`, `html`, `requests`, `smtplib`, `yaml`
- **Key symbols:** `_safe_html_truncate` (L55) - Truncates HTML without ending inside an entity or a tag, backing the cut off to the last safe boundary (lines 69-78). | `_read_telegram_enabled` (L81) - Reads alerts.telegram.enabled from config_dir/system_config.yaml, FAIL-OPEN to True on any error (lines 95-102). | `_SlidingWindowRateLimiter` (L109) - Thread-safe max-N-per-60s limiter; acquire(timeout=None) keeps the old unbounded wait, a timeout makes it give up (lines 119-145). | `ChannelConfig` (L161) - One channel: chat_id_env, label, enabled (default False) — the chat id is resolved from the environment at send time (line 622). | `SendResult` (L173) - The six-field result: success, tier, delivered_to, failed_to, sentinel_path, failed_log_written. | `TelegramNotifier.__init__` (L196) - Validates token and at least one of chat_ids/channels, builds the rate limiter, and sets the whole-send wall-clock deadline (default 8.0s) (lines 229-264). | `TelegramNotifier.from_env` (L271) - Builds a notifier from TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN + TELEGRAM_CHANNEL_PRIMARY, returning None if either is missing, and honours the config master switch (lines 288-297). | `TelegramNotifier.send` (L348) - The single public entry: master-switch short-circuit, then tier dispatch, then the audit line (lines 375-396). | `_audit_send` (L398) - The send-side audit trail — one info `alert_send` line carrying severity/source/title/outcome/delivered_to/failed_to; fully wrapped so it can never break a send (lines 422-444). | `_handle_critical` (L450) - Sentinel first (line 495), Telegram second (line 505), failed_alerts.log if any channel failed (line 509), SMTP email fallback if all failed (line 522). | `_post_with_retry` (L654) - One chat_id with the deadline-bounded ladder: rate-limit token, HTTP POST, retry on Timeout/5xx, Retry-After on 429 capped at 5s, permanent fail on other 4xx (lines 672-758). | `_send_email_fallback` (L797) - SMTP send (starttls + login + sendmail) using env-resolved from/password/to addresses; returns False when not configured (lines 801-851).

#### `ops.control_tower.aggregator` - `ops/control_tower/aggregator.py`

**Role:** aggregator-orchestrator | **Lines:** 323 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The Control Tower detection + lifecycle orchestrator: four monitor adapters (security last_run.json, cron heartbeats/markers, config auditor, excursion reconstruction runs) plus the freshness engine and the disk/backup check, all converted into Finding objects (lines 54-186, 220-230). run_aggregation (line 190) UPSERTs every finding, auto-resolves categories that ran but no longer detect, writes one control_tower_runs row, computes the health score and status roll-up, then hands the result set to the reporter for the Telegram delta and the HTML/CSV pull report (lines 245-292). Called by ops.control_tower.runner and by its own CLI main (line 298).

#### `ops.control_tower.db` - `ops/control_tower/db.py`

**Role:** persistence | **Lines:** 146 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The Control Tower DAO: all SQL for the five control_tower_* tables in one file. It UPSERTs findings on the dedup identity (category, resource_name, reason) without ever touching first_seen, re-opens a RESOLVED finding that is seen again, replaces a day's freshness rows, auto-resolves findings only in categories that actually ran, and writes the single run row (lines 27-146). Used by the aggregator and by the ack/unack CLI.

- **Imported by (5):** `ops.control_tower.aggregator`, `ops.control_tower.cli`, `tests.unit.test_control_tower_phase1b`, `tests.unit.test_control_tower_phase1c`, `tests.unit.test_control_tower_phase1d`
- **Imports locally (0):** none
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `ensure_indexes` (L22) - creates the idempotent idx_ct_findings_last_seen index (DDL at line 16) | `upsert_finding` (L27) - INSERT ... ON CONFLICT(category,resource_name,reason) DO UPDATE that refreshes last_seen/severity/location/action and flips RESOLVED back to OPEN clearing resolved_at | `replace_freshness` (L61) - DELETEs the run_date's freshness rows then executemany-inserts the new set | `prior_status_map` (L74) - returns {(category,resource_name,reason): status} for the pre-UPSERT delta the reporter needs | `auto_resolve_stale` (L81) - marks OPEN/ACKNOWLEDGED findings RESOLVED when their category ran but last_seen < scan_time; returns rowcount and no-ops on an empty category set | `ack_finding` (L97) - sets status ACKNOWLEDGED + acked_at for a non-RESOLVED id and commits | `unack_finding` (L105) - returns an ACKNOWLEDGED finding to OPEN, clearing acked_at, and commits | `get_findings` (L116) - selects id/severity/category/resource_name/reason/first_seen/last_seen/status/acked_at/resolved_at for the given statuses, ordered by the severity rank then id | `open_severities` (L126) - list of severity strings for status='OPEN' — the health score's input | `write_run` (L131) - inserts one control_tower_runs row from a dict of the 11 named columns | `_SEV_ORDER` (L113) - the CRITICAL..INFO tuple interpolated into get_findings' CASE ordering | `_INDEX_DDL` (L16) - the single extra 1b index tuple, described as not part of the v40 schema
- **Inputs:** an open sqlite connection supplied by the caller (every function's first parameter); Finding dataclass instances plus a scan_time ISO string (line 27); freshness row dicts carrying stage/expected_by/actual_at/delay_minutes/status (lines 66-70); a run dict with the 11 named run columns (line 131); a finding id and an ISO `now` for ack/unack (lines 97, 105); a status tuple for get_findings (line 116)

- **Side effects:** DDL execution (line 24); INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on control_tower_findings, _freshness, _runs (lines 31, 63, 64, 90, 98, 106, 134); commits inside ack_finding (line 101) and unack_finding (line 109)
- **External calls:** sqlite3 execute/executemany only — no HTTP, broker or subprocess calls in this file
- **DB tables:** `control_tower_findings` (L33), `control_tower_freshness` (L63), `control_tower_runs` (L136)

#### `ops.control_tower.disk` - `ops/control_tower/disk.py`

**Role:** check-module | **Lines:** 57 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Turns the Phase-1a size measurements into Control Tower findings: disk usage at or above 95% becomes CRITICAL and at or above 85% becomes HIGH, and backup/log directory growth beyond 500MB/300MB versus the most recent earlier control_tower_trends row becomes MEDIUM (lines 15-45). Growth checks are skipped entirely when no prior trend row exists, so no "grew since nothing" finding is possible (lines 38-41).

- **Inputs:** evaluate(conn, root, db_path, today) (line 21); the size dict from size_logger.compute_sizes — disk_used_pct, backup_size_mb, log_size_mb (lines 24, 27, 43, 45); the newest control_tower_trends row with date < today (lines 38-40)
- **Outputs:** a (sizes, findings) tuple (line 46): the size dict unchanged plus zero or more Finding objects with category disk or backup
- **Side effects:** filesystem reads only, through size_logger.compute_sizes (line 24); no writes in this module — the comment at line 22-23 says the caller may UPSERT the trend row
- **External calls:** shutil.disk_usage and directory walks indirectly via size_logger.compute_sizes (line 24)

The per-stage data-freshness engine. For candles, signals, orders, executions, reconstruction and eod it decides OK / OVERDUE / MISSING / NA from today's table contents plus (for candles) the fetch marker's mtime, and emits HIGH for MISSING and MEDIUM for OVERDUE (lines 54-117). #### `ops.control_tower.runner` - `ops/control_tower/runner.py`

**Role:** entry-point | **Lines:** 137 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

The single entry point the 17:05 Mon-Fri cron invokes (docstring lines 3-4; registered as `control_tower` in config/cron_registry.yaml). It runs the Phase-1a size-logger first so today's trend size columns exist for the report's 7-day backup trend, then the aggregator with the same `now`, passing SELF_JOB so the in-flight run is never flagged as a missed cron job (lines 63-70). It skips non-trading days with a SKIPPED heartbeat to avoid false MISSING freshness findings, and always records a SUCCESS/FAILED heartbeat with a matching exit code (lines 101-133).

#### `utils.cron_heartbeat` - `utils/cron_heartbeat.py`

**Role:** ops instrumentation / heartbeat writer | **Lines:** 209 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The heartbeat utility every cron script calls to record that it ran: record_heartbeat (55) writes one row through StateStore.insert_cron_heartbeat with an IST timestamp, HeartbeatTimer (98) is the context manager that times a job, marks it FAILED on an exception and optionally fires a per-job alert, and skip_if_non_trading_day (176) records a SKIPPED heartbeat so market-day-only jobs can return early. F2 encodes a separate FUNCTIONAL status inside the free-form message as '[func=STATUS] ...' (32-41) with no schema change.

- **Imported by (44):** `ops.control_tower.runner`, `ops.control_tower.size_logger`, `reports.daily_report`, `reports.daily_trade_review`, `scripts.auto_refresh_token`, `scripts.backup_restore_drill`, `scripts.backup_retention`, `scripts.capture_metrics_baseline`, `scripts.check_cron_drift`, `scripts.compute_strategy_metrics` ... (+34)
- **Imports locally (4):** `alerts.cron_alerts`, `core.state_store`, `core.time_authority`, `utils.holiday_guard`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `_encode_functional` (L35) - prefixes the message with '[func=<status>]' when a functional status is given, else returns the message unchanged | `parse_functional_status` (L44) - inverse of the encoder: reads the marker back out of a message, or None | `record_heartbeat` (L55) - returns False if the DB file is absent (81), else opens a StateStore, inserts the heartbeat with now_ist(), closes and returns True; never raises (94) | `HeartbeatTimer` (L98) - context manager holding job_name, db_path, execution status, functional_status, message, alert and critical flags | `HeartbeatTimer.__enter__` (L135) - records the start monotonic-ish time.time() and the started IST timestamp | `HeartbeatTimer.__exit__` (L140) - computes duration, sets FAILED plus the exception text on an exception (142-144), records the heartbeat, optionally alerts, and returns False so exceptions propagate | `skip_if_non_trading_day` (L176) - uses utils.holiday_guard.is_trading_day, falling back to weekday() < 5 on any error (198-199); on a non-trading day records a SKIPPED heartbeat and returns True
- **Inputs:** job_name, status (SUCCESS|PARTIAL|FAILED|SKIPPED per the docstring at 70), duration_sec, message, functional_status, db_path default data_store/trading_system.db (61); HeartbeatTimer(job_name, db_path, alert, critical, config_dir default 'config') (116-123); skip_if_non_trading_day(job_name, config_dir, db_path) (176-180)
- **Outputs:** one cron heartbeat row per call (85-91); bool: True when recorded, False on any error (93, 95); bool from skip_if_non_trading_day (208, 209); the functional status string from parse_functional_status (52)
- **Side effects:** opens a StateStore against the live trading DB and inserts a heartbeat row (84-91); on the alert path calls alerts.cron_alerts.alert_job_result, which dispatches the operator alert (158-169); mutates its own instance fields only; no global state
- **External calls:** SQLite write via core.state_store.StateStore (84-91); operator alert dispatch via alerts.cron_alerts.alert_job_result (160); holiday calendar read via utils.holiday_guard.is_trading_day (197)
- **DB tables:** `cron_heartbeat` (L85)
- **Unknowns:** the cron_heartbeat table's columns and constraints (the literal INSERT is in core/state_store.py, not here); which jobs are registered as critical (resolved inside alerts/cron_alerts.py from a registry under config_dir); whether any caller checks record_heartbeat's False return; whether a functional status containing ']' would be parsed correctly (the parser stops at the first ']', 49)

#### `utils.instance_lock` - `utils/instance_lock.py`

**Role:** process-guard | **Lines:** 257 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Single-instance enforcement for the trading process: an OS-owned exclusive lock on a lock file (authoritative, dropped by the kernel when the process dies) plus a persistent socket bound to a lock port (docstring 4-13, 133-138). main.py calls acquire_instance_lock() at boot (main.py:2191) and release_instance_lock() on the refusal path (main.py:2198); the docstring states main exits 1 on refusal and that a wrong refusal would become a restart loop (7-10).

- **Imported by (4):** `main`, `scripts.reconstruct_excursions`, `tests.unit.test_instance_lock`, `tests.unit.test_phase17_batch3`
- **Imports locally (0):** none
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `ctypes`, `fcntl`, `msvcrt`
- **Key symbols:** `acquire_instance_lock` (L129) - opens/creates the lock file (152), takes the OS lock (157), writes its PID fixed-width (172), then binds+listens on 127.0.0.1:lock_port (185-187); returns (True, "") or (False, reason) | `release_instance_lock` (L220) - closes the lock socket then calls _release_file_lock; documented as promptness only, not correctness (223-226) | `_release_file_lock` (L205) - unlocks and closes _lock_fd but deliberately never unlinks the lock file (206-208) | `_lock_fd_exclusive` (L88) - msvcrt.locking of one byte at offset 1024 on win32 (97-98) or fcntl.flock LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB elsewhere (100-101); False on OSError (103-104) | `_unlock_fd` (L107) - releases the byte-range/flock lock, swallowing OSError (116-117) | `_read_holder_pid` (L120) - reads the first 64 bytes of the lock file for the operator message, returning 'unknown' on failure | `_pid_is_alive` (L68) - PID liveness via ctypes OpenProcess on win32 (73) or os.kill(pid, 0) (80); PermissionError counts as alive (84-85) | `check_port_available` (L241) - connect_ex probe: reports the port busy iff a connection succeeds (252-254)
- **Inputs:** lock_port: int argument, default the module constant _LOCK_PORT = 5001 (55, 129); host/port for check_port_available (241); the TEMP environment variable on win32, to place the lock file (50); the existing content of the lock file (read as the holder PID, 124)
- **Outputs:** (bool, reason) tuples from acquire_instance_lock (145-146, 155, 163-166, 197-200) and check_port_available (254-255); the lock file itself, carrying this process's PID padded to 64 bytes (172)
- **Side effects:** creates/opens the lock file at %TEMP%/trading-system.lock or /tmp/trading-system.lock (50-51, 152); writes the PID into that file (172); the file is never unlinked (205-208); holds a kernel file lock for the process lifetime via the retained fd (176); binds and listens on 127.0.0.1:<lock_port>, never accept()ing (185-187); mutates the module globals _lock_socket and _lock_fd (148, 176, 180, 195, 217, 236); sets SO_EXCLUSIVEADDRUSE on win32 (181-184)
- **External calls:** socket bind/listen on 127.0.0.1 (185-187); socket connect_ex probe in check_port_available (252); Windows kernel32 OpenProcess/CloseHandle via ctypes (73-75)
- **Config keys read:** `TEMP` (L50), `_LOCK_PORT` (L55)
- **Unknowns:** whether the deployed systemd unit really uses RestartPreventExitStatus "3 4" as the docstring asserts (7-9) — not visible in this file; whether the platform measurements quoted in the docstring (26-33) still hold on the current hosts; who, if anyone, passes a non-default lock_port

#### `utils.startup_checks` - `utils/startup_checks.py`

**Role:** boot-preflight-library | **Lines:** 1,692 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

A read-only library of pre-trading boot checks that main.py calls in Phase 0c/0d: it classifies the startup into COLD/WARM/CRASH/HALT from the session row plus kill_switch state (line 192), then runs individual checks (clock vs broker, NTP drift, config-file presence, config-hash diff, scanner URL reachability, local webhook /health, instrument-cache size, strategy YAML validity, holiday calendar, kiteconnect version pin, TEMP markers, disk space, DB file permissions) and aggregates them into one StartupReport with `blocking_failures` and `warnings` (line 1508). It takes every dependency as a parameter (state_store, kill_switch, time_authority, broker_adapter, app_config, http_fetcher_fn) and its header states it performs no state mutations and no process control (lines 10-12, 31-36) -- the caller decides what to do with ok=False. Two of its checks break that read-only rule by raising StartupCheckFailed (lines 1003, 1059).

(+3)
- **Imports locally (3):** `core.exceptions`, `strategies.loader`, `strategies.schema`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `yaml`
- **Key symbols:** `StartupScenario` (L65) - Enum of the four boot scenarios: COLD (new trading day), WARM (same day clean stop), CRASH (same day, no SHUTDOWN marker), HALT (kill_switch was active). | `detect_startup_scenario` (L192) - Reads the session row and kill_switch.status(), and returns a StartupScenarioResult: HARD_KILL -> HALT (243), same-day SOFT_KILL -> HALT (263-286), different/absent prior date -> COLD (290), same day with a SHUTDOWN event -> WARM (311), same day with an EOD squareoff row whose status is COMPLETE -> WARM (346), otherwise CRASH (374). | `check_clock_skew` (L414) - Calls broker_adapter.get_server_time() (436) and time_authority.assert_clock_at_startup() (437); ClockSkewTooLarge yields passed=False with the skew from exc.context (445-458), any other exception yields passed=False with skew 0.0 (459-468). | `check_ntp_sync` (L506) - Queries an NTP host over UDP/123 with a hand-built 48-byte v3 packet via _query_ntp_time (475) and compares the transmit timestamp to time.time(); drift >= block_sec logs CRITICAL and sets passed=False (549-555), a failed query returns passed=True/skipped=True so boot is not blocked (537-545). | `check_config_hash` (L581) - Compares app_config.file_hashes (595) against the JSON dict stored in session.last_config_hash (598-602); blocking failures are missing_config_files, missing_secrets, halt_requires_resume, clock_skew, instrument_cache_too_small, invalid_strategy_configs, sdk_version_mismatch, ntp_clock_skew, insufficient_disk_space, db_permission_error (1553-1660).
- **Inputs:** Injected objects, all as parameters of run_all_startup_checks (1509-1526): state_store, kill_switch, time_authority, broker_adapter, market_windows, app_config, scan_webhook_map, chartink_scanners, http_fetcher_fn, webhook_url, required_secrets, config_dir, logger, instrument_cache, min_instrument_rows (default 1000), db_path, log_dir, min_free_disk_gb (default 1.0); today_date derived from time_authority.now_ist().date() (1548); Session row fields session_date (230) and last_config_hash (598); kill_switch.status() dict keys state / reason / triggered_at (238-240); Files on disk: required config filenames under config_dir (869), config_dir/nse_holidays_<year>.yaml parsed with yaml.safe_load (1396-1397), config_dir/strategies/*.yaml via StrategyLoader (1284), config_dir.parent/requirements.txt (1320-1326), config_dir/*.yaml and config_dir/strategies/*.yaml scanned for the literal '# TEMP' (1477-1482); Environment variables named in required_secrets, read via os.environ.get (1083); NTP host string (default 'pool.ntp.org', 508; passed literally at 1634) and thresholds warn_sec=2.0 / block_sec=5.0 (1635-1636); Installed distribution metadata for package 'kiteconnect' (default at 1312, read at 1338)

#### `v3_chain.forward_shadow` - `v3_chain/forward_shadow.py`

**Role:** research-pure-math | **Lines:** 105 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Pure recompute + path-simulation helpers for the EOD forward-shadow research recorder. It re-derives the four M-S4 step values, folds them into a recomputed score, bins a score into the study's coarse band, and walks a 1-minute path to a realised R. The header states it is RECORDS-ONLY research with no I/O, no clock, no broker, no config and no mode branch (lines 3-19); scripts.forward_shadow_record does the reading and appending.

- **Imported by (2):** `scripts.forward_shadow_record`, `tests.unit.test_forward_shadow`
- **Imports locally (0):** none
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `ms4_step_values` (L29) - returns {volume_surge, atr_filter, rsi_range, sector_strength} from real inputs, defaulting to 0.0 (or 0.5 for a missing RSI, 46-47) rather than fabricating | `ms4_score` (L55) - merges the recomputed four values over the persisted step_results and returns round(Σ weight·value) (62) | `score_band` (L65) - bins a score: '0-34' below 35 (69-70), '60-65' at or above 60 (71-72), else a 5-wide band (72) | `simulate_true_path` (L75) - walks (high, low, close) candles from entry, returning -1.0 on the SL touch, +tgt_r on the target, or the squareoff R off the last close (104-105); None for no candles or a degenerate entry (85-89)
- **Inputs:** volume, ltp, direction, avg_volume_20d, atr14, rsi14, sector plus the min_volume_surge / min_adr_pct thresholds (29-39); old_step_results and a weights dict (55-56); a score float (65); entry, direction and a chronological sequence of (high, low, close) tuples, with sl_pct / tgt_r (75-77)
- **Outputs:** Dict[str, float] of four step fractions (52); an int recomputed score (62); a band label string (70-72); a realised-R float or None (96-105)

- **External calls:** none

#### `v3_chain.models` - `v3_chain/models.py`

#### `v3_chain.pb01_entry` - `v3_chain/pb01_entry.py`

**Role:** shadow-entry-monitor | **Lines:** 371 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The stateful next-morning front end of PB-01 (V3 Step 10b): a daemon poll thread that, inside the configured entry window, watches each of today's pb01_watchlist rows for the first valid closed 5-minute retest confirmation of yesterday's broken level, and drives the row to a terminal status. On confirmation it calls the injected on_confirm callback (the place-free would-be runner); the header states it holds no order/reserve/place capability of any kind (lines 24-26). Constructed by main only when watchlist.enabled (main.py:3778).

#### `v3_chain.runner` - `v3_chain/runner.py`

**Role:** shadow-enrichment-worker | **Lines:** 425 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The V3 Step 10a async shadow enrichment worker. observe() is a fire-and-forget enqueue on the signal hot path; a background thread then fetches structure timeframes, truncates them to the signal's decision instant, builds S&R zones, evaluates the RR/HTF/EXTREME gates, composes the Context+Execution score and appends one would-be JSONL row. The pure 3-layer score COMPOSER for the V3 chain: it re-composes the existing screener step values (never rescoring them) into Playbook-40 / Context-40 / Execution-20, folding in the V3 regime, S&R-target and HTF factors and grouping correlated members so they cannot each take a full weight. #### `v3_chain.truncate` - `v3_chain/truncate.py`

**Role:** no-lookahead-filter | **Lines:** 111 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The single anti-lookahead chokepoint for the V3 shadow chain: given a candle series and a decision instant, it keeps only bars that had fully CLOSED at or before that instant, so the async worker's verdict carries no hindsight. Used by runner, pb01_runner and pb01_entry; the header calls it the mandatory correctness requirement of the Step-10a addendum (lines 1-15).

- **Imported by (4):** `tests.unit.test_v3_chain`, `v3_chain.pb01_entry`, `v3_chain.pb01_runner`, `v3_chain.runner`

- **Key symbols:** `interval_minutes` (L37) - bucket minutes for an intraday interval from the _INTERVAL_MINUTES map, None for daily/unknown | `_align_tz` (L43) - attaches or strips tzinfo so a naive-IST and an aware-IST value compare, never converting wall-clock | `close_ts` (L54) - bar start + interval for intraday; for daily, the next midnight after the bar's date (67-68) | `truncate_to_asof` (L71) - for daily, keeps bars whose DATE is strictly before as_of's date (89-94); for intraday, keeps bars whose close_ts <= as_of (95-97); preserves input order | `last_close_ts` (L101) - ISO string of the latest bar close in the series, for the record's audit trail; None when empty
- **Inputs:** a candle sequence exposing .ts (and nothing else), an interval string and an as_of datetime (54, 71, 101)
- **Outputs:** Optional[int] minutes (40); a close-instant datetime (65, 68); a filtered list preserving order (94, 98); an ISO string or None (111)
- **Side effects:** none — pure, stdlib only
- **External calls:** none
- **Config keys read:** `_INTERVAL_MINUTES` (L23), `DAILY_INTERVALS` (L34)
- **Unknowns:** whether the fetcher's candles are tz-aware, which decides if the _align_tz path is ever exercised; the exact Candle type (only .ts is touched here); whether any caller passes an interval outside the _INTERVAL_MINUTES map

#### `v3_chain.watchlist_capture` - `v3_chain/watchlist_capture.py`

**Role:** eod-capture-worker | **Lines:** 244 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The EOD side of PB-01 (V3 Step 10b): a background worker that takes an 'eod' scanner's breakout symbol, computes the broken LEVEL itself from its own settled daily candles (never the payload price), stamps the next trading day, records the nearest 30m/1h S&R zone and inserts ONE pb01_watchlist row. Skips a breakout on a still-forming daily session and relies on the store's UNIQUE(symbol, trading_date) to dedupe repeated firings (lines 1-18). Constructed by main only when watchlist.enabled (main.py:3769); the webhook EOD route calls submit().

#### `scripts.agy_runner` - `scripts/agy_runner.py`

**Role:** llm-subprocess-runner | **Lines:** 245 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Shared helper that shells out to an external `agy` CLI binary to run LLM prompts, with a hard-coded model cascade and quota-error detection. The six `scripts/gemini_*.py` reporting scripts import its per-task wrappers (run_watchman, run_log_review, run_trade_coach, run_weekly_patterns, run_data_integrity, run_premarket_brief) instead of calling a model API directly. Every failure path returns None rather than raising, so a caller sees 'no analysis' rather than an exception.

#### `scripts.auto_refresh_token` - `scripts/auto_refresh_token.py`

**Role:** entry-point / broker-auth | **Lines:** 420 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

Scheduled (cron) headless Zerodha login: resolves the primary account's credentials from env vars named in config/accounts.csv, POSTs user_id+password then a pyotp TOTP to kite.zerodha.com, walks the connect/login redirect chain for a request_token, exchanges it for an access_token and saves the canonical token JSON. It is the top of the boot chain, so it alerts on Telegram and records a cron heartbeat on both success and failure.

Category-aware reaper for data_store/backups: keeps the newest 20 pre_*.db, 14 trading_system-*.db and 14 analytics-*.db by mtime and deletes the rest, resolving SQLite -wal/-shm sidecars against their base .db. #### `scripts.check_cron_drift` - `scripts/check_cron_drift.py`

**Role:** cron-integrity monitor | **Lines:** 244 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

Daily 18:00 integrity check with two independent passes: PASS 1 lists monitored, due-today jobs with no execution signal in 24h (a heartbeat row for heartbeat_db jobs, a fresh cron_marks/<name>.done for exit_code_file jobs), and PASS 2 diffs the live `crontab -l` against generate(cron_registry.yaml) normalised through the generator's own parse/compose, bidirectionally. It then Telegrams a severity-tagged report and records its own heartbeat so the Cron Officer can watch the watcher.

#### `scripts.compute_strategy_metrics` - `scripts/compute_strategy_metrics.py`

**Role:** analytics / strategy scoring | **Lines:** 352 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

EOD job (cron 16:15 IST per docstring line 11) that, for every strategy with a CLOSED trade in the last 30 days, computes an annualised Sharpe from daily net_pnl sums, a win rate and an average P&L over a lookback window, and INSERT OR REPLACEs one strategy_metrics row per strategy per date. #### `scripts.cron_officer` - `scripts/cron_officer.py`

**Role:** monitoring orchestrator / entry-point | **Lines:** 952 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

The cron monitoring authority: it loads config/cron_registry.yaml as the roster, reads today's cron_heartbeat rows plus data_store/cron_marks/*.done, classifies every job due today by its detection method, and renders/routes either the morning briefing or the EOD report (HTML email sentinel and/or Telegram MarkdownV2). It also supervises the security watcher's state file, auto-discovers live crontab lines absent from the registry, and runs a four-part ROSTER INTEGRITY block including a Telegram getMe liveness probe.

#### `scripts.db_retention` - `scripts/db_retention.py`

**Role:** persistence / retention | **Lines:** 230 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

Row-level retention for the state store: for each of six tables it deletes rows whose date column is strictly older than a per-table cutoff (candles/system_metrics/webhook_audit/screener_results 90d, reconciliation_log 180d, fm_ledger 365d), each table in its own transaction so one failure does not abort the rest. It is the DB analogue of the log-file cleanup and is meant to run after the nightly backup (docstring lines 5-29).

- **Imported by (1):** `tests.unit.test_db_retention`
- **Imports locally (3):** `core.logger`, `core.state_store`, `core.time_authority`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `DEFAULT_RETENTION` (L63) - the policy map table -> (date column expression, retention days) | `_cutoff` (L73) - date_iso minus N days as 'YYYY-MM-DD' | `run_retention` (L80) - the prune loop; returns {table: rows deleted} plus a _failures count | `_parse_args` (L157) - --db, --date, --dry-run, --vacuum, repeatable --set TABLE=DAYS | `_parse_overrides` (L176) - validates TABLE=DAYS against DEFAULT_RETENTION, SystemExit on anything else | `main` (L195) - opens the StateStore, runs retention, returns 1 if any table failed
- **Inputs:** --db (default data_store/trading_system.db), --date (default today IST), --dry-run, --vacuum, --set (lines 162-172); an injectable policy dict and overrides dict (run_retention:85-86)
- **Outputs:** {table: rows_deleted_or_-1} plus results['_failures'] (lines 127, 130, 153); per-table db_retention.<table>_pruned / [dry-run] log lines (lines 113, 123); a db_retention.complete record with total_pruned/failures/dry_run (line 134); exit 0, or 1 when the store cannot open or any table failed (lines 206, 223, 226)
- **Side effects:** DELETE statements inside store.transaction, one transaction per table (lines 118-121); optional VACUUM of main and of the attached analytics schema, which takes an exclusive lock (lines 143-146); opens/closes a StateStore (lines 203, 222, 225); sys.path mutation (lines 45-46); no Telegram, no heartbeat, no file writes
- **DB tables:** `candles` (L64), `system_metrics` (L65), `webhook_audit` (L66), `screener_results` (L67), `reconciliation_log` (L68), `fm_ledger` (L69)
- **Log calls by level:** info 5 | error 4
- **Unknowns:** Whether `candles` and `system_metrics` are reachable from this connection -- scripts/backup_restore_drill.py:70-72 states they were relocated to analytics.db, and this script opens a StateStore rather than core.db_connect.; Whether the tables actually have the O4 STORED `date` column the policy assumes (lines 55-57).; Whether any cron entry runs this, with what flags -- this file has no cron guard and records no heartbeat, so the Cron Officer's heartbeat_db detection cannot see it.

#### `scripts.eod_broker_reconcile` - `scripts/eod_broker_reconcile.py`

**Role:** scheduled-entry-point / broker-vs-local reconciler | **Lines:** 478 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

Standalone 15:58 IST cron job that re-verifies the trading day against the BROKER with its own credentials, so the day is checked even if trading-system.service was down (docstring lines 4-8). It queries Zerodha positions/orders/margins/day-realized P&L (gather_broker, line 216), compares them to a read-only local snapshot (gather_local, line 249) in a pure verdict engine (compute_verdict, line 108), and persists a per-dimension verdict plus a pnl_reconciliation row (persist, line 288). Its only importer is tests.unit.test_eod_broker_reconcile.

- **Imported by (1):** `tests.unit.test_eod_broker_reconcile`
- **Imports locally (8):** `alerts.telegram_notifier`, `core.account_registry`, `core.config_loader`, `core.logger`, `core.state_store`, `core.time_authority`, `scripts.reconcile_positions`, `utils.cron_heartbeat`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `dotenv`, `kiteconnect`

- **Inputs:** argv: --date, --account, --db, --mode paper|live (lines 330-333); TRADING_MODE env fallback (line 344); config via core.config_loader.load_all() (line 343); broker credentials from scripts.reconcile_positions._resolve_credentials(account, config dir) (lines 378-379); local DB: data_store/trading_system.db by default (line 356); .env loaded at import (line 53)

- **Side effects:** DB writes: upsert_eod_broker_reconciliation (line 295), upsert_pnl_reconciliation (line 308); Broker network calls in LIVE: kite.positions(), kite.orders(), kite.margins() (lines 225, 236, 242); Telegram: send_alert(level='CRITICAL') when authoritative and not VERIFIED, else send_info (lines 416, 418); cron heartbeat record_heartbeat('eod_broker_reconcile', duration_sec=...) (line 423); load_dotenv mutates process env at import (line 53); sys.path mutation at import (line 50)

15:55 IST cron that asserts the day closed out: counts trades still OPEN/PARTIAL and orders still PENDING for the date, and evaluates the broker-vs-system P&L from pnl_reconciliation. Imported by tests.unit.test_eod_verify_honest and tests.unit.test_fix137_batch10.

- **Side effects:** DB write: INSERT OR REPLACE INTO eod_verification (line 159); Telegram send_info of the summary (line 225); cron heartbeat record_heartbeat('eod_verify') (line 232); log.critical on open positions/pending orders (lines 136, 139)
- **External calls:** Telegram send_info (line 225)
- **Config keys read:** `TRADING_MODE` (L204)

- **Paper vs live:** Mode comes from --mode or TRADING_MODE at line 204 and is passed as is_paper into run_eod_verification (line 206). EOD research recorder that, for every scored signal of the day, appends one immutable JSONL record combining identity, the live score and band, a recomputed 'M-S4' score, the live ADMIT/REJECT decision and reason, a simulated true-path R outcome and the realised net P&L for signals that traded. #### `scripts.generate_crontab` - `scripts/generate_crontab.py`

**Role:** config-generator / drift-gate | **Lines:** 407 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

The executable half of the self-maintaining cron design: compose() turns one registry job's generation fields into an exact crontab line, parse() turns a live crontab line back into those fields, and the two are asserted to be byte-exact inverses by --selftest. It also emits the canonical crontab from config/cron_registry.yaml, performs the one-time bootstrap of the registry from a live crontab, and runs a set-based gate comparing generated vs live. Imported by scripts/check_cron_drift.py and scripts/cron_officer.py plus four unit tests.

- **Imported by (5):** `scripts.check_cron_drift`, `scripts.cron_officer`, `tests.unit.test_check_cron_drift_content`, `tests.unit.test_cron_officer_phase3`, `tests.unit.test_generate_crontab`
- **Imports locally (0):** none
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `yaml`
- **Key symbols:** `compose` (L72) - builds one crontab line: cron_expression + env prefix + command + log redirect + marker block | `parse` (L91) - decomposes a live line into cron_expression/env_wrapper/command/log_target/marker_name | `_command_lines` (L137) - strips blanks, comments and the SHELL= line from crontab text | `selftest` (L147) - parse->compose each live line, asserting byte equality and compose determinism; prints and returns 1 on any failure | `resolve_job_name` (L215) - maps a parsed live line to its registry job name; markers win, then a special-case table, then script basename | `_index_by_name` (L238) - name->fields index over a crontab text; SystemExit on a duplicate resolved name | `bootstrap` (L249) - merges existing registry metadata with live generation fields, SystemExit on any registry job missing from live | `emit_registry_yaml` (L301) - serialises the enriched jobs+officer dicts with a fixed explanatory header | `load_jobs` (L313) - reads config/cron_registry.yaml into a list of job dicts with 'name' injected | `generate_canonical` (L323) - emits the canonical crontab text for enabled jobs only, with a per-job comment line | `gate_check` (L335) - set difference of generated vs live lines; fails on any drop or on any addition other than sentinel_retention | `main` (L360) - argparse dispatch across --selftest / --bootstrap / --generate / --gate
- **Inputs:** config/cron_registry.yaml (default at line 368); deploy/cron/trading-system.cron (default at line 369); a live `crontab -l` dump passed as --crontab FILE (line 367); the fixed deployment constants PROJ / VENV_PY / CLAUDE_DIR / MARKS (lines 42-45)
- **Outputs:** canonical crontab text on stdout, or ASCII+LF bytes to --out FILE (lines 386-395); enriched registry YAML on stdout for --bootstrap (line 383); SELFTEST PASSED/FAILED and GATE PASS/FAIL report lines plus exit codes (lines 166-173, 344-357)
- **Side effects:** writes the file named by --out, encoded as ASCII (line 392); prints report text to stdout (lines 166-172, 344-355); raises SystemExit with a STOP-AND-REPORT message on registry/live inconsistencies (lines 244, 273, 277, 282, 288)
- **External calls:** none -- no subprocess, no HTTP; the host paths at lines 42-44 are literal strings and are never invoked here
- **Config keys read:** `env_wrapper` (L74), `cron_expression` (L77), `command` (L78), `log_target` (L82), `marker_name` (L85), `jobs` (L317), `officer` (L255), `enabled` (L261), `schedule` (L329), `personal_tooling` (L262)
- **Unknowns:** Whether the pre-receive guard and the 'auto-install' arming described in the docstring actually exist anywhere; The content of the live crontab, so whether selftest/gate currently pass; Whether sentinel_retention is still the only legitimate registry-only addition (hardcoded at lines 275 and 347)

#### `scripts.liveness_probe` - `scripts/liveness_probe.py`

**Role:** service-liveness-alarm | **Lines:** 313 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

A 5-minute cron probe that alarms when trading-system.service is unexpectedly not active during [09:00, 16:00) IST on a trading day. It reads systemd state via `systemctl show`, exculpates a non-trading day, an out-of-window time, an active unit, an operator SOFT_KILL/HARD_KILL and an already-alarmed incident, and otherwise raises one CRITICAL over Telegram with a CRITICAL-sentinel fallback. It deliberately does not import main.py so it survives the failure it watches for.

- **Imported by (1):** `tests.unit.test_liveness_probe`

- **Inputs:** `systemctl show trading-system.service` properties (lines 211-216); config dir for the NSE holiday calendar (line 200); data_store/trading_system.db for the kill switch (line 201); the previous alarm state file under the configured sentinel dir (lines 233, 299)

- **Side effects:** subprocess `systemctl show` (lines 211-216); atomic write of liveness_alarm_state.json via the canary's _save_service_state (line 243); Telegram CRITICAL send (lines 276-278); CRITICAL sentinel file write as fallback (lines 285-286); load_dotenv at import (line 65); module-level logger creation (line 80)

A daily self-test of the alerting plumbing itself: it probes five independent paths -- SMTP login, Telegram getMe, alert_watcher sentinel ingestion, the gui-dashboard unit, and alert-watcher's restart rate -- and is quiet when healthy, loud over Telegram when any path fails, with a CRITICAL sentinel when Telegram is also down. liveness_probe reuses its systemctl parsing and state persistence helpers.

#### `scripts.output_retention` - `scripts/output_retention.py`

**Role:** retention-reaper | **Lines:** 315 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

A keep-N reaper for reports/output/ and logs/. Each glob family is counted independently, ranked by the YYYY-MM-DD embedded in the filename (never mtime), keeps the newest 7 and plans the rest for deletion; a run that would delete more than the cap aborts without deleting anything. Dry-run is the default. Imported by scripts/cron_officer.py (which reads the rule text) and two unit tests.

- **Imported by (3):** `scripts.cron_officer`, `tests.unit.test_evidence_contract`, `tests.unit.test_output_retention`
- **Imports locally (1):** `utils.cron_heartbeat`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `retention_rule` (L118) - renders the live rule sentence from the constants, for the cron report | `file_date` (L129) - extracts the YYYY-MM-DD from a filename, or None | `FamPlan` (L140) - per-family plan: keep list, delete list, bytes freed, undated-refused count | `Plan` (L152) - the whole plan with a refused list and to_delete / freed properties | `build_plan` (L165) - pure planner over the SCOPES table; refuses symlinks, undated files and files matching no family | `_assert_inside` (L215) - realpath assertion that a doomed path sits under its family's base dir | `render` (L221) - the human report: per-family counts, oldest kept, refusals, total and cap | `apply_plan` (L245) - deletes the planned files, per-file try/except, returns (deleted, freed) | `main` (L264) - argparse, plan, print, cap check, optional apply, then a heartbeat on both success and failure paths
- **Inputs:** CLI --apply, --keep (default 7), --max-delete (default 25) (lines 268-274); the directory listings of reports/output and logs, filtered by the SCOPES table (lines 102-115)
- **Outputs:** the OUTPUT RETENTION report on stdout (line 284); deleted files and (deleted, freed) counts (line 261); exit 0 on SUCCESS, 1 on FAILED (line 311); a cron heartbeat with status, duration and a deleted/freed message (lines 305-307)
- **Side effects:** unlinks files when --apply is passed and the cap is not exceeded (line 254); configures the root logging handler globally via logging.basicConfig (line 277); prints to stdout and logs warnings for every refusal (lines 284-286); records a cron heartbeat (lines 304-307)
- **Log calls by level:** info 1 | warning 3 | error 2
- **Unknowns:** Whether the crontab line actually passes --apply (this file defaults to dry-run at line 268); Whether reports/output still holds the retired daily_report_*.xlsx files; Which table utils.cron_heartbeat.record_heartbeat writes (line 305)

#### `scripts.preflight.base` - `scripts/preflight/base.py`

**Role:** contract-definitions | **Lines:** 126 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

The shared contract for every pre-flight check, kept deliberately dependency-light. It defines the criticality and status enums, the CheckResult / FixResult records, the CheckContext that the orchestrator injects (config dir, db path, mode, account, date, phase, dry_run, app_up, extra), and the Check base class with its NotImplementedError run()/fix() and four result-constructor helpers. #### `scripts.preflight.startup_hook` - `scripts/preflight/startup_hook.py`

**Role:** startup-recovery-hook | **Lines:** 96 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Called by main.py at process start: if the app comes up inside [06:00, 09:20) IST on a trading day, and the sentinel shows that phase A (08:30) or phase B (09:14) is past due but NOT_STARTED or stuck in RUNNING, it spawns scripts.preflight.orchestrator for that phase as a detached subprocess. It is a no-op on a normal morning because cron runs the phases regardless of the app, and it never raises into startup.

- **Inputs:** config_dir (default config), db_path (default data_store/trading_system.db), now, launcher and sentinel_path overrides (lines 71-76); the sentinel JSON via _sentinel.load (line 86)
- **Outputs:** the list of phase names launched (line 96); a warning log naming the launched phases (line 93)
- **Side effects:** subprocess.Popen of `python -m scripts.preflight.orchestrator --phase X --config-dir ... --db-path ...`, detached with output discarded (lines 63-67); logger warning on launch and logger error on any internal failure (lines 93, 95)
- **External calls:** subprocess.Popen of the orchestrator module (line 67)
- **Log calls by level:** warning 1 | error 1
- **Unknowns:** main.py's call site and whether it honours the returned list; Whether the orchestrator tolerates being launched concurrently with the cron-scheduled instance, and whether A and B running simultaneously (both can be spawned in one pass, lines 88-91) is safe; What the orchestrator's --watch-sec flag does, referenced at line 66 but defined elsewhere

#### `scripts.reconstruct_excursions` - `scripts/reconstruct_excursions.py`

**Role:** eod-backfill entry-point | **Lines:** 538 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

Post-EOD job that recomputes per-trade MFE/MAE into trade_excursions for closed trades, fetching missing 1-minute candles from Zerodha historical_data when needed. Every examined trade is classified WRITTEN / WOULD_FETCH / SKIPPED_UNRECONSTRUCTABLE / FAILED and a hard identity guard (written+would_fetch+skipped+failed == examined) makes the run fail loudly if a trade vanishes. #### `scripts.security_monitor` - `scripts/security_monitor.py`

**Role:** security watcher / alerting | **Lines:** 1,403 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Standalone, alert-only VM security watcher run by security-watcher.service roughly every 60s. Each pass runs ten isolated checks (authorized_keys hash/fingerprints, new login IPs, failed-login and root-probe spikes, non-whitelisted sudo, watched-file content hashes, active SSH session count with GeoIP enrichment, copy-protection ON->OFF, auditd copy-bypass, and the NSE holiday-calendar presence reminder), dedups findings through a presence ledger, and sends Telegram plus CRITICAL email sentinels. It is imported by approve_ssh_keys, copy_gate and system_manager for its SecConfig and helpers.

- **Imported by (8):** `scripts.approve_ssh_keys`, `scripts.copy_gate`, `scripts.system_manager`, `tests.unit.test_control_tower_phase1a`, `tests.unit.test_holiday_calendar_reminder`, `tests.unit.test_ni15_baseline_survives_none`, `tests.unit.test_security_monitor`, `tests.unit.test_security_monitor_realert`

- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `ipaddress`, `requests`, `yaml`
- **Key symbols:** `Finding` (L97) - dataclass severity/key/title/body; `key` carries the condition's identity and drives dedup | `SecConfig` (L109) - standalone config dataclass with defensive defaults, deliberately not the app's pydantic loader | `SecConfig.load` (L151) - overlays a whitelist of keys from the `security:` block plus the top-level `copy_protection:` block; never raises on a bad file | `apply_operator_ssh_baseline` (L186) - overlays data_store/security/ssh_key_baseline.json (outside git) which REPLACES the committed fingerprint/count | `scan_authlog` (L270) - one pass over auth.log lines collecting accepted IPs, failed count, root probes and sudo (user, cmd) | `established_ssh_connections` (L302) - `sudo -n ss -tnHp` parse into [{ip, pid}]; [] on any error | `geoip_lookup` (L376) - cached best-effort HTTP lookup at ip-api.com; 'local' for private addresses | `parse_ausearch_execve` (L475) - groups `ausearch -k copy_attempt -i` output by audit sequence id into {id, ts, exe, cmd} | `is_outbound_copy` (L510) - conservative heuristic distinguishing an outbound scp/sftp/rsync from an inbound sink-mode scp | `check_watched_files` (L721) - content-hash pass that RETAINS the baseline when a file is unreadable (NI-15 L1) and records why in state['file_unreadable'] | `_dedup` (L1146) - presence ledger: first report verbatim, persistence widens the interval on a 1x/4x/28x ladder and downgrades out of CRITICAL, an absence > gap counts as a new episode | `run_pass` (L1298) - runs the ten checks each in try/except and returns the pre-dedup findings

#### `scripts.sr_detector_backfill` - `scripts/sr_detector_backfill.py`

**Role:** shadow-outcome backfill entry-point | **Lines:** 149 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

EOD outcome backfill for the S&R detector shadow table: for each of the day's sr_detector_results rows it finds the most recent trade with the same signal_id and fills actual_fill / actual_result / win_loss / pnl. Only terminal trades are finalised; an open trade is left untouched so a later run picks it up, and a signal with no trade row at all is closed out as NO_TRADE/NO_FILL. hypothetical_retest_result is deliberately left NULL.

- **Imported by (1):** `tests.unit.test_sr_detector_backfill`
- **Imports locally (3):** `core.state_store`, `core.time_authority`, `utils.cron_heartbeat`

#### `scripts.strategy_registry_officer` - `scripts/strategy_registry_officer.py`

**Role:** registry maintainer entry-point | **Lines:** 305 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

Daily post-market maintainer of the strategy-direction registry. It enumerates the actual strategy set (loaded strategy YAMLs union the distinct signals.scanner/signals.strategy values), diffs it against the runtime state YAML in data_store (falling back to the git-tracked seed only on a first run), registers unseen strategies as PENDING, advances PENDING to CONFIRMED once a strategy has a filled trade, and marks health=DIRECTION_CONFLICT where a realized trade side differs from the declared direction. A no-change run is silent; it never changes a trading decision.

- **Imported by (2):** `tests.unit.test_registry_seed_state_split`, `tests.unit.test_strategy_registry_officer`

- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `dotenv`
- **Key symbols:** `_distinct_signal_names` (L53) - distinct non-null signals.scanner and signals.strategy values; a missing column is swallowed per column | `_filled_strategies` (L67) - distinct trades.strategy where qty_filled>0 - the CONFIRM trigger | `_realized_directions` (L81) - {strategy: {directions}} over filled trades - the CONFLICT input | `reconcile` (L98) - pure step returning (new_registry, newly_registered, newly_confirmed, newly_conflicted) | `_notify` (L172) - Telegram plus an email sentinel; silent when nothing to report, never raises | `run` (L222) - loads the direction map and registry, reads the DB if present, reconciles, saves on change, notifies; returns a summary dict | `main` (L276) - CLI --config-dir/--db-path/--registry/--dry-run; state path defaults under data_store; always returns 0 | `_cron_main` (L290) - skip_if_non_trading_day then HeartbeatTimer(alert=True) around main
- **Inputs:** CLI --config-dir, --db-path, --registry, --dry-run (lines 268-272); strategy YAMLs via core.strategy_direction.build_direction_map(config_dir) (line 230); runtime state YAML data_store/strategy_direction_registry.yaml (lines 231, 281-282); git-tracked seed config/strategy_direction_registry.yaml (line 283); signals and trades rows (lines 59, 72, 86)
- **Outputs:** summary dict {registered, confirmed, conflicted, total, changed} (lines 250-253); the rewritten registry YAML when changed (line 261); Telegram message and an email sentinel when there are registrations or conflicts (lines 201, 210); dry-run stdout line (line 256); exit code always 0 (line 287)
- **Side effects:** registry YAML write via save_registry (line 261); Telegram send (line 201); write_critical_sentinel into data_store (lines 210-215); DB connection open/close (lines 237, 243); load_dotenv of the repo .env at import (line 36); cron heartbeat row (line 295)

#### `scripts.strategy_status` - `scripts/strategy_status.py`

**Role:** report renderer (pure) | **Lines:** 221 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Builds the per-strategy 'will it trade today?' table from the same resolver the live entry gate uses (strategies.control.strategy_will_trade), so the operator report can never disagree with live behaviour. Renders three views of the same rows - a Gmail-safe inline-CSS HTML table, a compact Telegram view, and a plaintext mirror - plus a footnote naming DELIVERY strategies currently dormant. Consumed by scripts.cron_officer and two test modules.

#### `scripts.system_manager` - `scripts/system_manager.py`

**Role:** eod audit + soft-kill entry-point | **Lines:** 1,355 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

The 18:45 IST 'System Manager' EOD auditor. It runs twelve isolated checks over the trading DB, the config, the report/log tree and the VM itself (config-vs-actual caps, order quality, report integrity, system health, strategy health, risk events, today-vs-previous-trading-day, tomorrow readiness, copy-protection security, slippage overrides, stray .pyc, deployed-tree-vs-HEAD), builds one report, sends it to Telegram plus an email sentinel, saves it under reports/system_manager/, and trips SOFT_KILL for tomorrow only on a genuine safety violation.

- **Imported by (5):** `tests.unit.test_registry_seed_state_split`, `tests.unit.test_scheduled_report_severity`, `tests.unit.test_system_manager`, `tests.unit.test_system_manager_deployed_tree`, `tests.unit.test_system_manager_security`
- **Imports locally (13):** `alerts.critical`, `alerts.telegram_notifier`, `capital.kill_switch`, `core.account_registry`, `core.config_loader`, `core.events`, `core.logger`, `core.state_store`, `core.time_authority`, `scripts.security_monitor`, `scripts.zerodha_login`, `utils.cron_heartbeat` ... (+1)
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `dotenv`, `yaml`
- **Key symbols:** `CheckResult` (L69) - per-check accumulator with ok/warn/violation/info helpers; only violation() may set soft_kill_reason | `_effective_caps` (L141) - (max_open_positions, max_daily_trades) straight from system.risk - the live_test_mode swap was removed | `_day_capital` (L149) - the day's capital from the fm_ledger INIT row, falling back to accounts.csv paper_capital | `_max_concurrent_positions` (L171) - sweep-line over today's executed trades' entry/exit times for the peak concurrent count | `config_vs_actual_check` (L218) - checks peak positions, executed trade count, realized loss vs pct*capital, and max position value vs pct*capital; each breach sets a soft_kill_reason | `order_quality_report` (L304) - per-trade entry/exit slippage, gross/charges/net totals, partial fills and orphan SL/TGT legs | `system_health_check` (L421) - cron heartbeats, PRAGMA integrity_check (a failure is soft-kill-worthy), disk free, token validity, service starts/crashes, pending sentinels, kill-switch state | `tomorrow_readiness_check` (L662) - next trading day, stuck signals/trades/orphan orders, and whether a standing kill will auto-clear at the next 08:15 boot based on triggered_at's DATE | `security_check` (L811) - copy-audit tally plus auditd rule count, copy_protection flag, audit-log writability and security-watcher staleness; violations escalate the report but never soft-kill | `deployed_tree_check` (L1160) - diffs the deployed work tree against HEAD using a COPY of the repo index so the deploy repo is never written; also lists untracked .py | `trigger_soft_kill` (L950) - constructs KillSwitch(store, EventBus(), logger) and calls soft_kill unless a kill is already active | `main` (L1295) - CLI, config load, StateStore, run(), print+save report, optional soft-kill, send, heartbeat; exit 3 / 2 / 0
- **Inputs:** CLI --date, --config-dir, --db-path, --dry-run, --no-soft-kill (lines 1297-1301); the whole app config via core.config_loader.load_all (line 1318); config/security.yaml through SecConfig (line 823); config/strategies/*.yaml (line 510); config/accounts.csv (line 162); logs/system_<day>.log (line 201); reports/ and data_store/backups artefacts (lines 404-413); data_store/security_state.json mtime (line 805)
- **Outputs:** report text plus (violations, warnings, soft_kill_reasons) from generate_full_report (line 907); reports/system_manager/<day>.txt (line 942); Telegram message and an email sentinel every run (lines 926, 932); a cron_heartbeat row for 'system_manager_eod' (line 1343); SOFT_KILL state when reasons exist (line 960); exit code 3 / 2 / 0 (line 1349), or 1 for a missing DB / config failure (lines 1314, 1322)
- **Side effects:** writes reports/system_manager/<day>.txt (line 943); Telegram send and write_critical_sentinel on EVERY run (lines 926, 932); cron heartbeat DB write (line 1343); SOFT_KILL DB/state mutation via KillSwitch (line 960); subprocesses: sudo auditctl, git rev-parse/diff/ls-files (lines 783, 1191, 1196, 1206); creates a temp copy of the git index and deletes it (lines 1177-1180, 1250); mkdir of the copy-audit log's parent during the writability probe (line 795); load_dotenv of the repo .env at import (line 51)

- **Config keys read:** `system.risk.max_open_positions` (L146), `system.risk.daily_loss_limit_pct` (L263), `system.position_sizing.max_position_value_pct` (L279), `system.position_sizing.risk_per_trade_pct` (L296), `system.entry_gate.slippage_control.mode` (L977), `slippage_control.max_slippage_fraction` (L983), `slippage_control.overrides.enabled` (L984), `slippage_control.overrides.by_symbol` (L985), `system.alerts.sentinel_dir` (L1340), `security.yaml` (L823), `strategy` (L321), `accounts.csv` (L165)

#### `scripts.trade_journal` - `scripts/trade_journal.py`

**Role:** eod journal writer entry-point | **Lines:** 180 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

Generates one trade_journal row per CLOSED trade created on a given date, joining the trade to its signal (scanner, trigger_price), the screener score, and any trade_excursions MFE row. It derives entry_reason (scanner + score), a HIGH/LOW slippage assessment against trigger_price, and mfe_captured_pct, and is idempotent by deleting the date's journal rows first. Only tests.unit.test_fix133_trade_journal imports it.

- **Outputs:** count of inserted rows (line 144); trade_journal rows with date, trade_id, strategy, symbol, direction, entry_reason, exit_reason, slippage_assessment, mfe_captured_pct, entry_price, exit_price, net_pnl, notes, created_at (lines 129-140); one stdout line (line 160)

#### `scripts.wal_checkpoint` - `scripts/wal_checkpoint.py`

**Role:** db-maintenance entry-point | **Lines:** 70 | **Scheduled entry point:** yes

Tiny cron script that runs a PASSIVE WAL checkpoint on data_store/trading_system.db after market close so the write-ahead log does not grow without bound, then records a cron heartbeat. It prints the busy/log/checkpointed counters and exits non-zero on failure. Nothing imports it.

- **Imported by (0):** none
- **Imports locally (3):** `core.state_store`, `core.time_authority`, `utils.cron_heartbeat`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`
- **Key symbols:** `main` (L25) - resolves the DB path, exits 0 if it is absent, opens StateStore, calls checkpoint_wal(), prints the counters, records a heartbeat, exits 1 on any exception | `_cron_main` (L50) - S1 holiday guard (skip_if_non_trading_day, fails open) then main(); the guard is deliberately not inside main so a manual checkpoint still works
- **Inputs:** no CLI arguments; the DB path is hard-coded relative to the file (line 26)
- **Outputs:** one stdout line with busy/log/checkpointed (lines 35-38) or a stderr failure line (line 46); exit code 0 or 1 (lines 29, 47, 66); a cron_heartbeat record named 'wal_checkpoint' (line 42)
- **Side effects:** SQLite WAL checkpoint through StateStore.checkpoint_wal (line 33); cron heartbeat DB write (line 42); sys.path mutation at import (line 19); process exit via sys.exit inside main (lines 29, 47)
- **DB tables:** `cron_heartbeat` (L41)
- **Unknowns:** what StateStore.checkpoint_wal actually executes and what its busy/log/checkpointed keys mean; whether a crontab entry matching lines 8-10 is actually installed; whether record_heartbeat needs an explicit db_path here (none is passed, unlike reconstruct_excursions line 416)

#### `scripts.zerodha_login` - `scripts/zerodha_login.py`

**Role:** broker-auth helper + CLI | **Lines:** 288 | **Scheduled entry point:** no

Browser-assisted Zerodha session login: opens the Kite connect URL, takes a pasted request_token, exchanges it for an access_token via POST https://api.kite.trade/session/token with a SHA-256 checksum, and writes data_store/session/zerodha_token.json with owner-only permissions. It also provides the two token helpers (load_token, is_token_valid) that main.py, auto_refresh_token, healthcheck_server, reconcile_positions and system_manager all rely on.

- **Imported by (8):** `main`, `scripts.auto_refresh_token`, `scripts.healthcheck_server`, `scripts.reconcile_positions`, `scripts.system_manager`, `tests.unit.test_fix135_auto_token`, `tests.unit.test_h11_invalidate_token_path`, `tests.unit.test_zerodha_login`
- **Imports locally (2):** `core.account_registry`, `core.time_authority`
- **Third-party / external:** `__future__`, `dotenv`, `requests`, `webbrowser`
- **Key symbols:** `load_token` (L49) - parses the token JSON; returns None on OSError or JSONDecodeError | `is_token_valid` (L60) - true only if the file parses, account_id matches, the stored date equals today IST and access_token is non-empty | `exchange_request_token` (L84) - sha256(api_key+request_token+api_secret) checksum POST; raises RuntimeError on non-200 or a missing access_token | `save_token` (L132) - writes the record with os.open mode 0600 (plus chmod 0700 on the dir) and an expires_at of the next 05:00 IST | `run_login_flow` (L183) - interactive flow: open browser, read request_token via an injectable input_fn, exchange, save, return the access_token; sys.exit(1) on exchange failure | `_cli_main` (L239) - --account (required) and --token-path; resolves api_key/api_secret env names from accounts.csv, returning 5 on any resolution failure
- **Inputs:** CLI --account, --token-path (lines 244-251); config/accounts.csv via AccountRegistry.load, path relative to cwd (line 258); the account's api_key_env and api_secret_env environment variables (lines 267-268); a request_token pasted on stdin (line 222); the existing token JSON for load_token / is_token_valid (lines 54, 69)
- **Outputs:** access_token string returned by exchange_request_token (line 125) and run_login_flow (line 232); data_store/session/zerodha_token.json holding account_id, broker, access_token, api_key, date, saved_at, expires_at (lines 157-165); stdout instructions and status lines (lines 216-231); exit code 0 or 5 from _cli_main (lines 262-284)
- **Side effects:** creates data_store/session/ and chmods it 0700 (lines 144, 149); writes the token file with mode 0600 at creation and chmods it again (lines 170-176); opens the system web browser (line 214); HTTPS POST to the Kite session endpoint (line 104); load_dotenv() at import time (line 36); sys.path mutation inside _cli_main (line 255); sys.exit(1) from inside run_login_flow (line 228)
- **External calls:** webbrowser.open of https://kite.zerodha.com/connect/login?v=3&api_key=... (lines 40, 214); requests.post https://api.kite.trade/session/token with api_key, request_token, checksum (lines 41, 104-112)
- **Paper vs live:** none visible - this module always talks to the real broker endpoint; nothing here branches on a trading mode.
- **Unknowns:** whether Kite's real response nests access_token under 'data' or at the top level (both are accepted at line 120); whether the 05:00 IST expires_at matches Zerodha's actual token lifetime; what consumes the 'expires_at' field (nothing in this file reads it back - is_token_valid uses 'date' instead, line 75)

## 2.7 Verification accounting for this chapter

Descriptions, roles and the input/output phrasing were gathered by 30 parallel research agents reading the files; **every line number in this chapter was then resolved against the file itself** and each claim was graded. Nothing entered the chapter on an agent's word alone.

| Anchor class | Claims made | Anchored into the chapter | Dropped | Agent's own line was exact |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Symbols (class / function / method) | 1689 | 1687 | 2 | 1663 (98%) |
| Config keys | 691 | 667 | 24 | 613 (89%) |
| Database tables | 308 | 295 | 13 | 247 (80%) |

**How each class was anchored.** A **symbol** takes the definition line from the AST, so the citation cannot drift from the parser: 728 resolved to a top-level `def`, 220 to a `class`, 307 to a method inside its class, and 432 by a line scan. A **config key** or **table** keeps the agent's line only when the token really is on that line (613 and 247 cases); otherwise the first occurrence in the file is used (54 and 48). A **table** is only accepted if it is one of the 53 tables the schema files and the shadow database actually declare - 13 tokens failed that test and were dropped, not guessed.

**Defect observations are not in this chapter.** 682 code observations were raised while mapping; a defect belongs in chapter 22 and nowhere else, so they are parked with their file, line and the text of that line, and will be triaged there. A count is not a finding: none of them is a confirmed defect until chapter 22 examines it.

## 2.8 What this chapter could not establish

- **Runtime truth.** Import reachability is not execution. Which of the 200 mapped modules actually run on a given day, and in what order, is chapter 03's question and is answered there only as far as the code and the schedules show.
- **The three dynamic-import sites** of SS2.1 are outside the static walk, so a module loaded only through them would not appear in the runtime set at all.
- **Whether a NOT MAPPED module is dead.** Nothing here establishes that. Several are plainly live in other roles - the `ops_dashboard/` tree is a separate process with its own unit file (chapter 25).
- **Production.** The mapping is the testing VM's tree. Whether production's tree matches it file for file is NOT ESTABLISHED; the measured divergences that are known appear in chapter 01's appendix.
- **24 config-key tokens and 13 table tokens** could not be anchored and were dropped rather than guessed; they are listed in the chapter's working data, not here.
